# COMPLETE — Lunavera Coffee

Everything that is finished, moved here from the old `TODO.md`. **443 `[x]` marks** (counted 2026-11-12 over the whole file: sections 0 to 7 and the finished parts of section 1) plus the audit history and the old section 8. Item numbers and wording are unchanged, so a reference such as "bug 2.23" still points to the same text. Items that are still open are in `TODO.md` (see its header). The earlier versions of this line gave 331, 332 and 374; they counted different things and were replaced by this one.

How to read it: "done" means done in code and tested, as written in each item; the checks that need a person, a real printer or a real server stay in `TODO.md` section 0. Section 1 blocks below show only what is done; their open parts (1.1, 1.3, 1.4, 1.5, 1.6, 1.7) are in `TODO.md` under the same heading, and 1.8 is only there.

Moved out on purpose, they are open in `TODO.md`: the "Birthday" idea in section 7 (it asks for an automatic discount or free drink and nothing is given automatically), and items 2.37, 2.62, 2.85, 4.26, 4.30 and 6.41, which were ticked here before three re-audits and a merge scan (2026-11-01) showed part of what they ask for is missing. **Update 2026-11-07:** all of them are ticked again and are back in this file (see the clean-up note in the history below).

## 1. Requested by the owner (2026-10-10)

### 1.1 Fix the receipt QR so scanning shows readable text  — priority: high, small — **DONE in code (session 35), waiting for a phone scan**
- [x] The scan result looked like jumbled, "encrypted" text. Fixed: the QR text is now ASCII only (see the checked steps below). **Still to do by the owner: scan a new receipt on the phone and tick the last box.**
- Likely cause **(inferred, verify by scanning)**: `ReceiptQr.payload()` builds text with Vietnamese letters and the đ sign
  ("Hóa đơn", "Tổng", "25.000 đ"). `QrCode` writes it as UTF-8 bytes but adds no character-set marker (no ECI segment).
  Many phone scanners then read the bytes as Latin-1 and show garbage such as `HÃ³a Ä‘Æ¡n`. The QR itself is valid; the
  text is just decoded in the wrong character set.
- Fix plan:
  - [x] Make the payload ASCII-only (for example `Lunavera Coffee` / `Hoa don #12` / `Tong: 25.000 VND` / `14:30 10/10/2026`),
        since plain ASCII reads the same on every scanner. Optional extra: add a UTF-8 ECI marker to `QrCode` too.
  - [x] Keep it a plain summary. It must not be a payment request (see 1.4).
  - [x] Add a test that the payload contains only ASCII characters (`ReceiptQrTest`: ASCII only, one byte per character, amount format, locale independent). Decoding is covered by `QrCodeTest`; I also checked the rendered image with OpenCV by hand.
- Files: `util/ReceiptQr.java`, `util/QrCode.java`, `controller/HoaDonController.java` (draws the image).

### 1.2 Rename uploaded product images to a unique name  — priority: medium, small — **DONE (session 36); confirmed working on Windows by the owner, 2026-10-10**
- [x] Before: `ImageStorage.storeNewFile` named the copy `System.currentTimeMillis() + "_" + originalName` and copied with
      `REPLACE_EXISTING` **(confirmed)**. Two uploads in the same millisecond with the same original name could silently
      overwrite one another, and the original file name (spaces, Vietnamese letters, very long names) was kept in the name.
- What was done:
  - [x] The stored file is now named `<timestamp>-<random UUID>.<extension>`, for example `1760100000000-3f2b8c1e-....jpg`. The original name is dropped.
  - [x] Only the extensions `png`, `jpg` and `jpeg` are accepted (any capitalisation; stored in lowercase). Anything else (`.gif`, `.webp`, `.exe`, no extension, a trailing dot) *(Outdated since bug 2.58: `.jfif`, `.jpe`, `.gif` and `.bmp` are accepted too, JFIF/JPE stored as `.jpg`, 8 MB limit, see 2.58 and 3.12.)*
        is refused **before** a byte is copied, with the message "Chỉ hỗ trợ ảnh PNG hoặc JPG". The product dialog already shows that message.
  - [x] The copy never replaces an existing file (it fails with "file already exists" instead). If the generated name is taken it tries a new one, up to 5 times, then gives up with an error.
  - [x] Old files and old-style names (`1728500000000_latte da.png`, old absolute paths, `seed_*.png` from the starter menu) still resolve; nothing was renamed or moved. The starter-menu seeding was left alone (it uses fixed `seed_<name>` names on purpose, so a re-run reuses them).
  - [x] The product dialog now shows the name of the file you picked, because the stored name is a random string and would mean nothing to the person using the dialog.
  - [x] Tests (`ImageStorageTest`, 6 new, 12 in total): two uploads of the same file give two names and two files; the original name does not appear in the stored name (plain, accented and 150-character names);
        only PNG/JPEG are accepted; a forced name collision does not overwrite the existing file and retries with a new name; the class gives up when every name is taken; an old-style name still resolves.
        I ran these 12 here with a small stand-in test runner (no Maven access); they all passed. Run `mvn test` on your machine to confirm with real JUnit.
- Owner check: [x] on Windows, a new picture gets a name like `<number>-<letters and digits>.jpg` and still shows in the table. **Confirmed working, 2026-10-10.**
- Not changed here (tracked elsewhere): the old picture is still deleted at the moment a new one is picked, before Save (bug 2.21), and the Vietnamese-letter test names are skipped on a machine whose file system cannot create them.
- Files: `util/ImageStorage.java`, `ImageStorageTest.java`, the product dialog in `AdminController` (label only).

### 1.3 Keep the data inside the project instead of the user's home folder  — priority: high, medium — **DONE in code (session 37), waiting for the owner's check**
- [x] Before: the database, pictures, logs and backups lived in `~/.lunavera-coffee` (`C:\Users\<name>\.lunavera-coffee` on Windows) **(confirmed)**, written as separate `user.home` paths in five classes.
- What was done:
  - [x] **One place decides where the data is:** the new `util/DataPaths`. The data folder is, in this order: the `-Dlunavera.dataDir=...` system property, the `LUNAVERA_DATA_DIR` environment variable, otherwise **`data/` inside the folder the app is started from** (the project folder with `mvn javafx:run`). A relative value counts from the start-up folder. A blank value is ignored.
        `DatabaseConfig`, `ImageStorage`, `AppLogging` and `MoneyMigration` all ask (and `DataCleanup`, removed in session 40) `DataPaths` now; nothing else builds a path by hand. The folders are `data/lunavera.db`, `data/images/`, `data/logs/`, `data/backups/`.
  - [x] **`data/` is in `.gitignore`**, with a comment, so the database (customer phones, password hashes) cannot be committed by mistake.
  - [x] **One-time copy from the old folder** (`DataPaths.migrateLegacyDataIfNeeded`, called before the first database connection): if the new folder has no database yet and `~/.lunavera-coffee/lunavera.db` exists, the database (copied to a temp name, then renamed, so a half-copied file never counts), the `images/` and the `backups/` are **copied**; the old folder is never changed or deleted. Existing files in the new folder are never overwritten. A line in the log says what was copied.
        Logs are not copied (history, not data). A marker file `data/.migrated-from-home` records the move, so deleting `data/lunavera.db` later to start fresh does **not** quietly bring the old data back.
  - [x] **If the old database cannot be copied** (disk full, permissions), the app stops with a clear message instead of starting with an empty database that would look like all the shop's data was lost; the old data is untouched. (Pictures and backups that fail to copy are only logged: copy them by hand.)
  - [x] **Old picture paths keep working:** products saved with a full path to the old folder still show, because that folder is left in place.
  - [x] **Backups:** the save dialog now opens in your user folder, not in `data/`; saving a backup inside `data/` shows a warning (a backup next to the live data is not a real backup); and the success message names the real database file instead of `~/.lunavera-coffee` (this also fixes bug 2.28).
  - [x] **Backing up onto the live database file is refused** (bug 2.23, first part), because the database now sits in a folder that is easy to browse to; see the update under bug 2.23.
  - [x] Tests: `DataPathsTest` (12: setting, relative setting, default, blank setting, "inside the data folder", copy of database/pictures/backups with the old folder untouched, runs only once, deleting the new database does not bring the old one back, never overwrites an existing database, nothing to copy, existing pictures kept, new folder = old folder) and `BackupSafetyTest` (3: the live file is refused and the database stays intact, a backup to another file is a valid database, the database is in the configured folder).
        All database tests now point `lunavera.dataDir` (and `user.home`) at a temp folder, so no test can touch your real `data/` or read your real old folder. I ran `DataPathsTest` here (12 passed) with a stand-in test runner; the database tests need SQLite and Maven, so they are **not run here**.
- Things to know:
  - The default `data/` is relative to where the program is **started**. `mvn javafx:run` starts in the project folder, so it works. A packaged build or an IDE run configuration may start somewhere else: set `-Dlunavera.dataDir="D:\LuneveraData"` (or the environment variable) for those, and the data will not move when you rebuild.
  - Cleaning (`mvn clean`), switching git branches and re-extracting the zip do not delete `data/` as long as you extract the new zip **over** the old project folder, or copy `data/` into the new one. Extracting into a new empty folder starts a new `data/` (the old one is in the old project folder, and the original data is still in `~/.lunavera-coffee`). This is written down in the new `README.md`.
  - The backup button (and the automatic backups in `data/backups/`) still keep copies on the same disk. Copy backups to a USB stick or another machine regularly (see 2A.4 and 6.9).
  - The pictures of the starter menu are stored with fixed names, so a re-run reuses them (unchanged by this item).
- Not done here (tracked elsewhere): backup bug 2.23 other parts (tmp-then-rename, `.db` extension), the quiet fallback when the folder cannot be created (2.26), restore from the app (4.1).
- Files: `util/DataPaths.java` (new), `config/DatabaseConfig.java`, `util/ImageStorage.java`, `util/AppLogging.java`, `config/MoneyMigration.java`, `AdminController` (backup dialog), `.gitignore`, `README.md` (new), `DataPathsTest`, `BackupSafetyTest`, and the tests that set `user.home`.

### 1.4 Bank transfer / bank card payment with a QR image named `qr-transfer`  — priority: medium, medium — **DONE in code (session 38) except the picture itself: the owner must supply the QR image**
- What was done:
  - [x] **New payment method "Chuyển khoản"** (`PaymentMethod.TRANSFER`). The database column is plain text, so there is no schema change and old orders read as before. Each method now carries its screen name in one place (`label()`), and the history list and the receipt use it instead of their own copies of "Tiền mặt" / "Thẻ".
  - [x] **Payment dialog:** a third choice next to "Tiền mặt" and "Thẻ". Choosing it shows the amount to transfer in large type, the shop's QR picture (240 px), the order code to use as the transfer note (`DH000012`, when the order already exists), and a tick box **"Tôi đã nhận đủ tiền trong tài khoản"**.
        The confirm button changes to "Đã nhận tiền - hoàn tất" and the order **cannot be paid until the box is ticked** (so pressing Enter by habit cannot complete a sale whose money has not arrived). "Tiền khách đưa" is only for cash now.
  - [x] **Picture missing:** instead of a QR the dialog shows "Chưa có ảnh QR chuyển khoản. Hãy đặt ảnh tên "qr-transfer.png" vào thư mục: <data folder>". The sale can still be completed once the box is ticked (the customer may pay from the account details).

  - [x] **The QR picture is not printed on the receipt.** The receipt only names the method ("Chuyển khoản"); the receipt's own QR is the plain summary from 1.1, not a payment code, so a paid receipt cannot be scanned and paid again.
  - [x] **Report and history:** the report totals add up revenue from every paid order whatever its method, so transfer sales are counted without a change there (checked by a test); the history list shows "Chuyển khoản" and sorting by payment still works.
  - [x] Tests: `PaymentMethodTest` (5) and `TransferQrTest` (7: finds the picture, any capitalisation, jpg/jpeg, png preferred, ignores look-alike names and folders, missing folder, data-folder URL, nothing is made up) were run here and pass.
        New database tests in `OrderDAOTest` (4): a transfer order is saved and read back, paying an open order by transfer stores the method, **cancelling a transfer order puts stock and points back**, and transfer sales count in the report totals. These compile but need SQLite, so they are **not run here**; run `mvn test`.
- Things to know: the dialog window resizes when "Chuyển khoản" is chosen, which I could not see on screen, so please look at it (section 0). There is no automatic check that money arrived (that needs a bank connection); the tick box is the cashier's confirmation. A card payment ("Thẻ") still records only the method, with no approval code.
- Files: `model/PaymentMethod.java`, `util/TransferQr.java` (new), `OrderController` (payment dialog), `HoaDonController`, `OrderHistoryWindow`, `PaymentMethodTest`, `TransferQrTest`, `OrderDAOTest`.

### 1.5 A way to edit the database directly (like phpMyAdmin), and maybe another database  — priority: medium — **DONE in code (sessions 42-44): MariaDB/MySQL is now the app's default database (phpMyAdmin works on the shop's data), SQLite is an option and an hourly snapshot copy; waiting for the owner's check on a real server**
- **Decision (owner, 2026-10-11): option C, MariaDB/MySQL with phpMyAdmin.** Options A (an external SQLite tool) and B (a built-in "Database" page) stay discarded.
- **Assumptions I made because the owner's questions were not answered (tell me if any is wrong):** MariaDB (a MySQL server works with the same driver and SQL); one server on the shop PC, with every till pointing at it by host name; a Windows install with XAMPP is acceptable (the guide is written for it); no connection pool yet (see "Not done").
- **How it is switched (changed in session 44):** MariaDB/MySQL is the **default**. With no `data/database.properties` the app looks for a server on this computer (`127.0.0.1:3306`, database `lunavera`) and, if there is none, stops at start-up with a message that says where to put the settings file (and that `db.type=sqlite` brings the old file back). `mvn test` is unaffected: the pom makes the tests run on SQLite unless `-Dlunavera.test.db=mariadb` is given.
  Earlier (sessions 42-43) SQLite was the default; the owner decided on 2026-10-13 to make MariaDB the default and keep SQLite as an option plus an hourly snapshot copy. The SQL that differs is in one small class (`SqlDialect`), and tests compare the two table definitions column by column so they cannot drift apart unnoticed.
- What was done:
  - [x] **Phase 1, the seam.** `DatabaseSettings` reads `data/database.properties` (host, port, name, user, password, optional SQLite import path, optional `mysqldump` path; every key can also be a `-Dlunavera.<key>` option; names and hosts are checked so nothing odd can reach the URL; the password is never written to a log or a message). `DatabaseConfig` connects per database, skips the SQLite-only `PRAGMA`s for MariaDB, and keeps the schema version in a `schema_version` table instead of `PRAGMA user_version`.
        Driver `org.mariadb.jdbc:mariadb-java-client:3.4.1` in `pom.xml` and `requires org.mariadb.jdbc` in `module-info.java`. If the server is down the start-up message now says so and names the settings file (`StartupProblem`).
  - [x] **Phase 2, the SQL.** Every item of the list in the old plan was handled: `AUTOINCREMENT` (a separate MariaDB table definition, `MariaDbSchema`: `INT` ids, `BIGINT` whole-dong money, real `DATETIME`/`DATE`, `VARCHAR` lengths, InnoDB, utf8mb4, indexes and the unique customer phone key);
        `DATE('now','localtime')` and `DATE('now','-6 days',...)` (the dashboard asks with a date from the app's own clock, which also removes any difference between the server's and the till's time zone); `VACUUM INTO` (becomes `mysqldump`); `INSERT OR IGNORE` and `COLLATE NOCASE` (spelled by `SqlDialect`); `MAX(0, x)` (a `CASE`); `PRAGMA user_version`, `table_info`, `sqlite_master`, `foreign_keys`, `busy_timeout` (`schema_version`, `information_schema`, nothing needed).
        Dates go through one helper, `DbTime` (ISO text in SQLite as before, `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss` for MariaDB; reading accepts both).
        **Other differences found while converting (not in the old list):** (1) `LIKE ... ESCAPE '\'` means something else in MySQL, which reads a backslash inside quotes as its own escape: the history search now uses `!` as the escape character in both databases (`escapeLike` and its test changed); (2) `CAST(x AS TEXT)` became `CAST(x AS CHAR)` (works in both); (3) a plain `TEXT` column cannot be a key in MySQL, so the small flags table and the unique columns use `VARCHAR`;
        (4) the unique customer phone from bug 2.7 is a partial index in SQLite, which MySQL does not have: in MariaDB mode a customer without a phone is stored as **NULL** (never `""`), which a plain unique key allows to repeat; `CustomerDAO` does that for both databases and recognises MariaDB's duplicate-key error 1062; (5) `SchemaMigrations` (indexes, unique phone) and `MoneyMigration` run only on SQLite, because a new MariaDB database is created with all of that.
  - [x] **Phase 3, moving the data and backing up.** `SqliteToMariaDbImporter`: put `db.import.sqlite=data/lunavera.db` in the settings file and the next start copies **everything** into the **empty** MariaDB database, keeping every id, converting the dates, turning blank customer phones into NULL, and checking that every table has the same number of rows. It is one transaction (any failure rolls everything back and the app stops with a message), it never runs if MariaDB already has data, and the SQLite file is only read.
        Backups: the "Sao lưu dữ liệu" zip and the daily automatic backup hold `lunavera.sql` (a `mysqldump`, the password sent in an environment variable, not on the command line) plus the pictures. **Restore is by hand** with phpMyAdmin's Import (the in-app "Khôi phục dữ liệu" is SQLite-only and now says how, instead of swapping a file the app would ignore).
  - [x] **Phase 4, the guide.** `docs/DATABASE.md` (install XAMPP, create the database and a user that is **not** root, the settings file, the import, rules for editing in phpMyAdmin, backups and restore, security, what each error means, the differences from SQLite, running the tests on MariaDB), `database.properties.example`, and the README. `data/` was already in `.gitignore`, so the password file is outside git.
  - [x] **Session 43 follow-up (what was left over).**
        (1) **"Mở phpMyAdmin" button** on the admin dashboard, shown only to an admin when the shop runs on MariaDB: it asks first (phpMyAdmin skips the app's checks: stock, loyalty points, order totals) and then opens `http://localhost/phpmyadmin/` in the browser; `db.phpmyadmin` changes the address, and only `http://` / `https://` addresses are accepted (a `file:` or `javascript:` link is refused when the settings are read).
        (2) **Plain Vietnamese messages when the database has a problem you can act on** (`DataAccessException`): server unreachable, wrong login (names `data/database.properties`), database busy. Before this, a stopped server in the middle of a sale showed only "Failed to ...". The technical text stays in brackets; every other error is unchanged.
        (3) **Numbered upgrades for an existing MariaDB database** (`MariaDbMigrations`, driven by `schema_version`; no step is needed today). It also **refuses to start an app that is older than the database**, so an old copy cannot damage newer tables.
        (4) **An SQL audit of every remaining query for MySQL 8:** one real problem found and fixed: the table list (`TableDAO.findAll`) selected a column that was not in its `GROUP BY`, which SQLite and MariaDB accept but MySQL 8 refuses; it now uses `MAX(id)` (the newest open order of a table). No other query needed a change.
        (5) **Bug 2.64 fixed** (the money migration of an old SQLite file no longer drops `table_number` and `discount_reason`); see that item.
  - [x] **Session 44: the owner's decisions of 2026-10-13, and the first real build.**
        **First real build (owner's `mvn clean test`):** it compiled with the MariaDB driver on the module path, which **confirms the module name `org.mariadb.jdbc` and the driver version**; 305 tests ran, 304 passed, and 1 failed: `FontBundleTest` (`java.awt.Font` could not be read: "module does not read java.desktop"). That test is from the font work (1.6), not from the database work; **fixed** by letting the test JVM (only) read `java.desktop` (`--add-reads` in the surefire `argLine`). The pom also pins surefire at 3.6.0, the version Maven was already using, which removes the "plugin version is missing" warning.
        **Carrying the old data over (owner's choice, 2026-10-13: "carry my old data over").** The import (`db.import.sqlite=data/lunavera.db`, now enabled in `database.properties.example`) works **at any time while MariaDB holds nothing but what the app creates by itself on a first start** (the `admin` account and its employee row, the 12 dining tables, the starter menu): then it clears those and brings the old data in, in one transaction, so the first start no longer closes the door on it.
        It is refused (log line, nothing changed) when MariaDB already has **real data**: any customer, any order, a second account or employee, or a product or category that is not part of the starter menu. A file without a `users` table is ignored with a warning before anything is touched. The old accounts come over, so you sign in with the **old** admin password. If it fails, everything is rolled back; the usual cause is two customers with the same phone (MariaDB allows one per phone), and the message names the number and says to fix or merge them in the SQLite version of the app.
        Not changed: the old `data/lunavera.db` (only read) and `data/images` (the pictures stay where they are).
        **MariaDB is the default** (above). **Hourly SQLite snapshots (`SqliteSnapshotService`):** while the shop runs on MariaDB the app copies every table into `data/snapshots/lunavera-yyyyMMdd-HHmmss.db` every hour it is open (the first about 30 seconds after start-up if the newest is older than an hour) and keeps only the **newest 10** (older ones are deleted; files that are not snapshots are never touched). One consistent view of the data (a `START TRANSACTION WITH CONSISTENT SNAPSHOT`), a row-count check per table, written under a `.partial` name and renamed when complete, a failure logged and retried an hour later, never stopping the app. The file is a complete SQLite database at the current version with its indexes, so it can also be used by the app itself (copy it over `data/lunavera.db`, `db.type=sqlite`); it holds no pictures. A SQLite shop gets no snapshots.
  - [x] **Tests on a real server are possible and safe.** `mvn test -Dlunavera.test.db=mariadb -Dlunavera.test.db.password=...` runs every database test on a MariaDB database whose name **must end in `_test`** (it drops every table in it first; any other name is refused, so the shop's database can never be wiped). Tests about SQLite files, PRAGMAs and the SQLite-only migrations skip themselves in that mode.
  - [x] **New tests, 57 (42 in session 42, 15 in session 43), the pure ones run here and passed:** session 43 added `DataAccessExceptionTest` (5), `MariaDbMigrationsTest` (6), two in `DatabaseSettingsTest` (the phpMyAdmin address) and two in `MoneyMigrationTest` (these two need SQLite, so they are compiled but not run here; I replayed their SQL in Python and it behaved). Session 42: `DatabaseSettingsTest` (10), `SqlDialectTest` (5), `DbTimeTest` (7), `MariaDbSchemaTest` (8: no SQLite-only words, tables created after the ones they point to, drop order, **same columns as the SQLite tables**, money is `BIGINT`, dates are `DATETIME`, login names exact, one phone per customer), `SqliteToMariaDbImporterTest` (6: the copied tables and columns equal the MariaDB tables, copy order is valid, date and blank-phone conversion), `MariaDbBackupTest` (2: the command, the password never on it), `BackupEntryNameTest` (3), and one in `StartupProblemTest`. The existing `escapeLike` test was changed for the new escape character.
- **Not done / limits (please read):**
  - [x] **Server down while selling:** the start-up message is clear, and a mid-sale failure now says in plain Vietnamese that the server cannot be reached (the rest of 5.15, translating every other message, is still open).
  - Behaviour that differs from SQLite on purpose: text compares ignoring accents and capitals (the categories `Trà` and `tra` count as one name, and a search for `ca phe` finds `Cà phê`); login names stay exact.
- Files: new `config/SqlDialect`, `DatabaseSettings`, `SqliteSchema`, `MariaDbSchema`, `MariaDbBackup`, `SqliteToMariaDbImporter`, `util/DbTime`, `docs/DATABASE.md`, `database.properties.example`; changed `DatabaseConfig`, `MenuSeeder`, `SchemaMigrations`, `OrderDAO`, `CustomerDAO`, `UserDAO`, `TableDAO`, `BackupService`, `StartupProblem`, `DataAccessException`, `MoneyMigration`, `HelloApplication` (host services), `AdminController` and `admin-trangchu.fxml` (restore message and the phpMyAdmin button), `pom.xml`, `module-info.java`, `README.md`, and the tests named above; session 43 also added `config/MariaDbMigrations`.
- **MERGED (session 45, 2026-10-15; 427 tests on SQLite, 399 on MariaDB after the staff-phone fix):** the owner's MariaDB branch (the version that carries the old SQLite data over and replaces the first-start admin) was merged with sessions 42-44. Checked here: the whole merged project compiles; **424 tests pass on SQLite and 395 on a real MariaDB 10.11 server** (the rest are SQLite-only by design); an old SQLite file with an admin, a customer and the starter menu was imported into an empty MariaDB database and into one that had only first-start data, and the first start then showed no first-run dialog and the old `admin` account (also when the import line is left in on later starts). Not checked: the driver in the pom (3.4.1; here 2.7.6), Windows, XAMPP, phpMyAdmin, MySQL 8 (only MariaDB), several tills at once. Also done in the merge: `orders.paid_by` (2.47) added to the MariaDB table, the importer and the money migration; `AppFlags` and the history search, sorting and "today" queries use the same SQL on both databases; the older copies of `ScreenFit`, `SceneNavigator`, `SessionGuard`, `PasswordChangeService`, `StartupProblem` in the owner's branch were replaced by the newer ones (the server hint of `StartupProblem` kept). Staff phone (2.17): fixed right after the merge (see below). Still SQLite-only: the in-app restore and the restore banner (item 1.8).

### 1.6 Fix the text font so it looks right on Windows (it only looks right on Linux today)  — priority: high — **done in code in session 39 (one bundled font on every window); waiting for the owner's Windows check**
- [x] Reported by the owner: the text font works on Linux but not on Windows. **Cause confirmed from the owner's screenshots (below): Georgia has no glyphs for many Vietnamese letters.**
- **DONE in session 39 (code):** the app now bundles **Be Vietnam Pro** (regular, italic, bold; SIL Open Font License, `resources/fonts/OFL.txt`) and every window uses it.
  - New `util/AppStyle`: loads the three font files and adds `css/app.css` (`.root { -fx-font-family: "Be Vietnam Pro"; }`) to the scene of **every** window that opens: screens, the code-built windows, all dialogs and alerts, menus and tooltips. Called once in `HelloApplication.start`. So the dialogs and the receipt window are covered without touching each one.
  - Every `-fx-font-family` (about 130 rules: Georgia, Serif, System, Segoe UI) was removed from the 7 style sheets, so the text inherits the one font. The chart tick labels name the font too.
  - `font-weight: 600` (25 places) became `bold` and `500` (4 places) became `normal`, because only regular, italic and bold are bundled and the medium/semi-bold files are separate font families to JavaFX. This makes semi-bold headings a little heavier.
  - **Checked here:** I ran real JavaFX with a virtual screen and a screenshot: the font loads, the screen, a button, an alert, bold and italic all resolve to Be Vietnam Pro, and `ẮẰẲẴẶ ỘỢỮỰ ỹỵ đĐ` and the digits draw correctly (the digit 0 is now a normal zero). `FontBundleTest` (4 tests) checks the font files hold every Vietnamese letter with every tone mark and that no style sheet names a machine-dependent font again.
  - **Not verified:** Windows itself. The icon symbols (`⌂ ▤ ▦ ● ☕ ✎ ✓ ← →` and others, see 2.34) are not in this font, so Windows has to borrow them from another font; on Linux that works. See the check in section 0. If an icon shows as a square, give those labels `Segoe UI Symbol`.
  - Left for later: 125% scaling check (bug 2.4), and the decision about replacing the text icons with drawn shapes (2.34).
- **Evidence from the owner's Windows screenshots (2026-10-10, 6 screens): the cause is confirmed.** On the **products, categories and accounts** screens every Vietnamese letter that carries a hook, a dot below or a stacked mark is drawn as an empty square, while the **dashboard, orders and customers** screens look right.
  - Broken (empty squares): `ả ạ ấ ầ ẩ ậ ẻ ẹ ế ề ệ ỉ ị ỏ ọ ố ồ ộ ớ ụ ủ ứ ừ ự` and also `ư` (examples seen: "Qu□n lý s□n ph□m" for "Quản lý sản phẩm", "T□t c□" for "Tất cả", "N□□c ép" for "Nước ép", "Ch□a ch□n" for "Chưa chọn", "Tr□ng thái" for "Trạng thái", "Bánh ng□t" for "Bánh ngọt", "Danh m□c" for "Danh mục").
    These are the letters from the Unicode block "Latin Extended Additional" (U+1EA0-U+1EF9) plus `ư`/`ơ` (U+01A0-U+01B0).
  - Fine on the same screens: `à á â ã è é ê ì í ò ó ô õ ù ú ý ă đ Đ` ("Cà phê", "Trà", "Đang bán", "Tên" all show correctly), so only part of Vietnamese is missing, which is why it looks like a random square here and there.
  - Which screens: exactly the three whose style sheets use **Georgia**: `quanlysanpham.css`, `quanlydanhmuc.css`, `quanlytaikhoan.css`. The screens that use "Segoe UI" or the default font (`admin-trangchu.css`, `quanlydonhang.css`, and the customers screen, which uses the dashboard sheet) show every letter. The sidebar (always Segoe UI) is correct even on the broken screens.
  - Cause: **the Georgia that ships with Windows does not contain these Vietnamese letters**, and JavaFX does not borrow them from another font. On Linux "Georgia" is not installed, so a default font with full Vietnamese coverage is used instead, which is why nobody saw it there.
  - A second, smaller effect visible in the same screenshots: in Georgia the digit 0 is drawn like a small letter "o" (old-style figures): the "0" in the statistics cards, the product counts and `48.000đ`. It does not break anything, but numbers look uneven.
- **Quick stopgap (about 10 minutes, then check each screen):** replace `Georgia` and `Serif` with `"Segoe UI"` in the four style sheets that use them (the three above plus `dangnhap.css` for the login screen), keeping `sans-serif` as the fallback. This fixes Windows at once (Segoe UI is shown correctly in all screenshots) but changes the look of those screens from serif to sans. The proper fix below makes both systems identical.
- What the code says today **(confirmed by reading the style sheets)**: fonts are chosen only by name, and the names differ by screen, so each machine picks whatever it has:
  - `"Segoe UI", sans-serif` — about 56 rules, the dashboards (`admin-trangchu.css`, `employee-trangchu.css`, `quanlydonhang.css`)
  - `"Georgia"` — about 55 rules, the login and the category, product and account screens
  - `"Serif"` and `"System"` — about 20 more rules, mixed in with the above
  - No font file is bundled (no `Font.loadFont`, no `resources/fonts`). Dialogs, alerts and the receipt have no style sheet, so they use the system default font.
  - On Linux neither Georgia nor Segoe UI is normally installed, so every name falls back to one default font and the whole app looks the same. On Windows the real fonts exist, so each screen gets a different typeface.
- Other things to keep in mind (not seen in the screenshots):
  - Screens mix Georgia (serif) and Segoe UI (sans), so the app looks like two different programs.
  - Symbols used as icons (bug 2.34) come from whichever font Windows picks; in the screenshots they show, but small.
  - The login screen, the employee dashboard, the dialogs and the printed receipt were not in the screenshots: check them too after the change (the login sheet uses Georgia, so it is probably affected).
- Plan (decide the typeface first):
  - [x] (assumed, not confirmed by the owner: one sans-serif, Be Vietnam Pro) Owner: choose the look: one sans-serif for everything (suggested: "Be Vietnam Pro", made for Vietnamese, or "Noto Sans"), or keep a serif for headings only. Both are free to ship (SIL Open Font License); check the licence text is included.
  - [x] Bundle the font files in `src/main/resources/fonts/` (regular, medium, bold) and load them once at start-up in `HelloApplication` with `Font.loadFont(...)`, so Windows and Linux use the same file.
  - [x] Replace the font rules in all 7 style sheets with one shared rule, for example a root rule `.root { -fx-font-family: "Be Vietnam Pro"; }` in one shared `app.css` that every screen loads, with `sans-serif` as the fallback. Remove the per-rule `Georgia`, `Segoe UI`, `Serif` and `System` names.
  - [x] Give dialogs, alerts and the receipt window the same style sheet (`AlertUtil.configure` and `SceneNavigator.loadAndShowNewWindow`), so they match the rest.

### 1.9 MariaDB: account names are only protected by the app, not by the database  — priority: low, small — **NEW (session 45, found while fixing the staff phone), DONE in code (session 115)**
- On SQLite a unique index (`ux_users_username_nocase`) makes `Admin` and `admin` the same account for the database itself. On MariaDB `users.username` is `utf8mb4_bin` (exact), so the database would accept both; only the app's checks (login compares lower-case, `AccountService` refuses a name that exists in any case) stop it, so editing the `users` table in phpMyAdmin, or two tills creating the same name at the same moment, could still create a pair.
- Fix options: a generated lower-case column with a unique key, or switch the column to a case-insensitive collation (which also ignores accents, so `an` and `ản` would collide, probably acceptable for login names). Needs a duplicate check first (skip with a warning, like the SQLite index), a migration for existing MariaDB databases, and a test on a real server.
- **DONE (session 115):** the first option. `users.username_key` is a stored generated column `LOWER(username)` (`utf8mb4_bin`) with the unique key `ux_users_username_key`, in `MariaDbSchema` for a new database and added by `SchemaMigrations.ensureMariaLoginNameKey` to an older one (the column first, then the key; if two names already differ only in capitals the key is skipped with a warning that gives the query to list them, and it is tried again at every start, like the SQLite rule and the staff-phone key). `username` itself stays `utf8mb4_bin`, so `mai` and `mái` are still different logins (the collation option would have merged them). The app's own check and message are unchanged: the server's `Duplicate entry ... for key 'ux_users_username_key'` contains "username", so `AccountService.translate` shows "tên đăng nhập đã có". Run for real on MariaDB 10.11 here (the DDL, the pair `Mai`/`mai`/`MAI` refused, `mái` allowed, the upgrade path with and without an existing pair, and a `mariadb-dump` / reload round trip); the JUnit class `MariaDbLoginNameKeyTest` (2 tests) runs in the `test-mariadb` CI job and was not run here (no MariaDB JDBC driver). `MariaDbSchemaTest` knows `username_key` is MariaDB-only. Not done: nothing needed on SQLite (it has the index since session 42).

[x] **[High] Typed money amounts are misread:** *(session 41: `util/MoneyParser` now exists and is used for the employee salary; the product price/cost, discount and cash-received fields still need switching to it)*  "25.000" becomes 25 đ.** **NEW (3rd scan)** The product dialog (`AdminController`, price and cost, around lines 1292 and 1302) and the payment dialog *(DONE in code, session 42: every money box (product price and cost, discount, cash received) now uses `MoneyParser`: `25.000`, `25,000`, `25000đ` work, letters and exponents are refused with a clear message.)*
        (`OrderController.parseNonNegative`, around line 983) all call `new BigDecimal(text)`. The app shows money as `25.000đ` (dot as thousands separator), so a person who types it the same way saves a price of 25 đ
        (`Money.toDong` rounds `25.000` to 25). `25,000` is rejected, `1e5` is accepted, and `100.5` is silently rounded. A cashier typing a discount or cash received hits the same trap. Fix: one shared
        `MoneyParser` that drops `.`, `,`, spaces and a trailing `đ`/`d`, accepts digits only, and rejects the rest with a clear message; use it in all four fields and add a test. **(confirmed in code)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan** with `new BigDecimal(text)` + `Money.toDong`: `25.000` becomes 25, `1e5` becomes 100000, `100.5` becomes 101, `25,000` is rejected with NumberFormatException. *(Name check 2026-11-08: `OrderController.parseNonNegative` is gone; money typed in a box is read by `MoneyParser` (this item's own fix).)*
17. [x] **[High] Login by phone number is ambiguous and case-sensitive.** **NEW (3rd scan)** `UserDAO.findByUsername` runs `WHERE u.username = ? OR e.phone = ? OR c.phone = ? LIMIT 1` with no `ORDER BY`. *(DONE in code, session 42: login tries the username first (any capitals), then the phone in normalised form, in a fixed order; usernames that look like a phone are refused; staff phones are normalised and unique (`AccountService` + index `ux_employees_phone`); usernames are unique ignoring capitals (`ux_users_username_nocase`). Existing duplicates only produce a log warning and the index is skipped.)*
        Employee phones are not checked for uniqueness and are saved un-normalised (`phoneField.getText().trim()` in `AdminController`), usernames may be made only of digits (4-32 characters), and
        `existsByUsername` looks at usernames only. So: two staff with the same phone, or a username equal to someone's phone, makes it arbitrary which account a login opens; an employee saved as `+84901234567` cannot
        log in with `0901234567`. Usernames are also case-sensitive (`Nam` and `nam` can both exist) while `LoginThrottle` ignores case. Fix: try an exact username match first; refuse usernames that look like a phone number;
        normalise and uniqueness-check employee phones (same rule as customers); decide on case-insensitive usernames (`COLLATE NOCASE` unique index, after checking for duplicates). **(confirmed in code)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** two employees with the same phone were both saved with no error and a login by that phone returned the lower id; `Nam` and `nam` were both created while a search for `NAM` found neither; a 10-digit all-digit username is valid (`isValidUsername` = true).

Add a test. **(confirmed in code; needs an old-style row to trigger)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** `ImageStorage.deleteQuietly(<absolute path of a file in the temp folder>)` deleted that file (it was outside the images folder).

        packaged Windows app has no console. So a crash in a button handler or a timer leaves nothing in `logs/`, and the user sees nothing. [x] **[Low] `DatabaseConfig.getConnection` leaks the connection when the set-up after connecting fails.** **NEW (11th scan)** On SQLite it opens the connection, then runs `PRAGMA foreign_keys`, `busy_timeout`, `synchronous` and registers the `VN_KEY` function; none of that is inside a `try` that closes the connection. If one of those calls throws (the file is locked longer than the timeout, a disk I/O error, a damaged file), the exception reaches the caller but the open connection (and its file handle, and in WAL mode its share of the `-shm` file) stays until the garbage collector finds it. On a shop PC that retries every 30 s (`SessionGuard`), a long fault piles up open handles, and on Windows an open handle blocks a backup restore or a file delete. **(confirmed in code)** Wrap the set-up in `try { ... } catch (SQLException | RuntimeException e) { connection.close(); throw e; }`; test with a connection source that fails on the pragma. *(DONE in code, Session 87: the set-up moved into `DatabaseConfig.configureSqlite`, which closes the connection when any step throws. Test: `DatabaseConfigConnectionTest` with a stand-in connection; run here: passed.)*

Today an admin-set hash is always made with the current count, so this is latent, and it becomes real the next time `ITERATIONS` is raised (the count was raised once already, 2.12). **(confirmed in code)** Give the re-hash its own method (`UPDATE users SET password_hash = ? Tests: `PasswordRehashTest` adapted (passed here) and `aSilentRehashKeepsTheMustChangeMarkButAChosenPasswordClearsIt` (needs SQLite, not run here).)*

If `mysqldump` hangs (a table lock that never frees, a network stall to the server on another PC), the thread never reaches the timeout: the dashboard's backup button stays disabled, and an automatic backup thread sits there. **(confirmed in code; a hang needs a fault to reproduce)** Start a small reader thread for the output (or redirect it to a file), call `waitFor(timeout)` on the main thread, and `destroyForcibly()` on a timeout; test with a fake `db.mysqldump` script that sleeps. *(DONE in code, Session 87: `MariaDbBackup.dumpTo` reads the output on a helper thread and waits for the program with the real limit; at the limit the program is killed and the partial file removed. The dump command now passes `--no-tablespaces` (MySQL 8.0.21+ otherwise needs the PROCESS privilege that a database-level user lacks); if the client is too old to know the option and says so (\"unknown option\", \"unrecognized option\" or \"unknown variable\" naming `no-tablespaces`) the same dump is run again without it; any other failure is not retried and removes the half file. Tested here with a stand-in `mysqldump` shell script (3 end-to-end tests: retried without the option; known option run once; another failure reported and no file left) plus 2 text tests: `MariaDbBackupTest` 7 pass (the process tests need a Unix shell). `docs/DATABASE.md` has a new troubleshooting row. [x] **[Med] A decimal amount typed in a money box is read as a number 10 or 100 times too big.** **NEW (15th scan)** `MoneyParser.parseDong` (2.16) drops every `.`, `,` and space wherever it sits, so it cannot tell a thousands separator from a decimal point. **Reproduced on this tree:** `25.5` and `25,5` give 255, `10.50` gives 1050, `1,234.5` gives 12345, `25,000.50` gives 2500050, `1.000,5` gives 10005, `1,2,3` gives 123. `parseDong` and `parseOptionalDong` serve the product price and cost, the discount, the cash received, the salary and the CSV import, so a price typed `25.5` (for 25.500) or a pasted `10.50` is saved without a word, 10 to 100 times too small or too large. 3.46 describes the same flaw only for the CSV stock column. Fix: accept a separator only when exactly three digits follow it (`25.000`, `1,250,000`); anything else with a `.` or `,` is refused with the existing message plus "không nhập số thập phân". Add the cases above to `MoneyParserTest`. *(14th pass: DONE. `MoneyParser` now accepts `.`, `,` and a space only as a thousands separator: one kind throughout, a first group of 1 to 3 digits not starting with 0, every later group exactly 3 digits. `25.000`, `1,250,000`, `8 000 000` (also with non-breaking spaces) and `50.000đ` are read; `25.5`, `25,5`, `10.50`, `1,234.5`, `25,000.50`, `1.000,5`, `1,2,3`, `1250.000`, `0.500` and mixed separators are refused with \"Số tiền chỉ được gồm chữ số (ví dụ 8000000 hoặc 8.000.000); không nhập số thập phân.\" (the optional form used by the discount and cash boxes returns null for them, which the screen already reports). 4 new `MoneyParserTest` cases, run here with the old ones and `ProductCsvTest`, `MoneyGuardTest`: 19 pass. [x] **[Med] When the disk is full, every sale fails with a message that says "try again".** **NEW (15th scan)** **Reproduced** on a 14 MB filesystem filled to the brim: 400 of 400 sales failed (nothing half-saved: no order, stock and points unchanged), with the cause `SQLITE_IOERR_SHMSIZE`. `DataAccessException.hintFor` knows a wrong password, a lost server and a busy database, but not a full disk, so the cashier sees only the generic "Đã xảy ra lỗi. Hãy thử lại; nếu lỗi vẫn còn, báo người phụ trách" and retries again and again. A full disk is handled at start-up (`StartupProblem`) and in the backup (free-space check), nowhere else. Fix: map `SQLITE_FULL`, `SQLITE_IOERR*` and MariaDB 1021/1114 to "Ổ đĩa đầy hoặc không ghi được. Hãy giải phóng dung lượng rồi thử lại; đơn vẫn được giữ trên màn hình"; see 4.24 for a warning before it happens (free space under 200 MB on the data drive). *(14th pass: DONE. `DataAccessException.hintFor` now recognises a full or unwritable disk and says \"Ổ đĩa đầy hoặc không ghi được, nên chưa lưu được. Hãy giải phóng dung lượng trên ổ đĩa chứa thư mục dữ liệu rồi thử lại.\" instead of the generic \"try again\": SQLite by result code (`SQLITE_FULL` or any `SQLITE_IOERR`, extended codes included, read from `SQLiteException.getResultCode().code & 0xff`), MariaDB by error 1021, 1114 or \"Got error 28\", and the operating system's own words as a fallback, anywhere in the cause chain. New `DiskFullHintTest` (6) beside the existing `DataAccessExceptionTest` (5), run here: 11 pass. NOT checked against a real full disk.)*

[x] **[Med] A database password with a backslash, a space at either end, or `\u` in it is silently changed, and a password ending in a backslash swallows the next setting.** **NEW (19th scan)** `DatabaseSettings.load` reads `data/database.properties` with `Properties.load` and then `.trim()`s every value. Run on the real class: `db.password=ab\cd` is used as `abcd`; `ab\\cd` as `ab\cd`; `x\u0041y` as `xAy`; `  lead` and `trail   ` lose their spaces; and `abc\` (a backslash at the end of the line) joins the next line, so the password becomes `abcdb.type=sqlite` and the `db.type=sqlite` setting under it is lost, which sends a SQLite shop to MariaDB. Characters like `#`, `!`, `=` and `:` are fine. The shop sees only "Access denied" or "cannot connect", with the password written correctly in the file. A password made by a generator, as `docs/DATABASE.md` advises ("use a long one"), can easily contain a backslash. **(reproduced by running `DatabaseSettings.fromProperties`)** Fix: read the file in a way that has no escapes (read the lines yourself, split at the first `=`, and trim only a trailing line break), or at least refuse a value that contains `\` with a message, and write in `docs/DATABASE.md` and `database.properties.example` that the password must not contain a backslash; add a test with the nine cases above. *(14th pass: DONE. `database.properties` is read literally: `DatabaseSettings.parseLiteral` splits each line at the first `=` and keeps the value exactly (a backslash is an ordinary character, `\\u0041` is not decoded, spaces at either end of the password are kept, a trailing backslash no longer swallows the next setting, so a SQLite shop is not sent to MariaDB); comment lines start with `#` or `!`; a Notepad byte-order mark and Windows line ends are handled; a password of only spaces still means \"no password\" (XAMPP). `-Dlunavera.db.password` is used as typed too. `DatabasePropertiesFileTest` (10), run here with `DatabaseSettingsTest`: 24 pass. Docs: `docs/DATABASE.md` and `database.properties.example` now say write paths with single backslashes (older files with doubled ones still work on Windows) and no comment after a value. **Behaviour change to know:** a value in the file is no longer trimmed for the password only; every other setting is still trimmed by `fromProperties`.)*

[x] **[Med] After a crash, a restore's safety copy (and the restore itself) can lose the last sales that were still in the write-ahead log.** **NEW (25th scan)** The database runs in WAL mode (6.16): committed sales sit in `lunavera.db-wal` until a checkpoint moves them into `lunavera.db`, and a power cut or a killed process leaves the `-wal` file behind. A restore that was parked before the crash is applied in `LunaveraApplication.init()` *before* the database is opened, so nothing has recovered the log yet: `BackupService.applyPendingRestore` writes the "before-restore" safety zip with `writeZip(safety, database, ...)`, which copies the bare `lunavera.db` file only (the `-wal` is not included), and then deletes `-journal`, `-wal` and `-shm` before moving the restored file in. Everything sold since the last checkpoint is therefore missing from the safety copy and is gone from the shop's disk, although the message promises "Dữ liệu trước đó được lưu tại ...". **(confirmed in code; the crash itself was not staged)** Before the safety copy, open the live file once (SQLite replays the log on the first connection) and take it with `VACUUM INTO` (the way `DatabaseConfig.backupTo` does) or run `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` and close; only then delete the side files. Test: write a row, copy the db and `-wal` pair to a folder without checkpointing, run the restore, and look for the row inside the safety zip. *(14th pass: DONE in code. `BackupService.applyPendingRestore` now opens the live file once (SQLite replays the leftover write-ahead log) and runs `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` BEFORE the safety zip is written and before the side files are deleted (`foldLeftoverLogInto`); if the file cannot be opened the `-wal` file is kept in `data/backups/` as `lunavera.db-wal.unfolded-<time>` instead of being deleted. Checked the mechanism on real SQLite (Python): a sale only in the log is missing (0) from a plain copy of the main file and present (1) after the fold. 3 new `BackupServiceTest` cases (a staged crash with a sale only in the log ends up in the safety copy and the restored database is the backup; folding with no log is a no-op; an unfoldable log is kept) compiled, NOT run (need the SQLite driver).)* *(Unticked in the audit of 2026-11-01: the main part holds, a sale that is only in the log does reach the safety copy and the restored database is the backup (two of the three new `BackupServiceTest` cases pass on real SQLite). The third case, `aLogThatCannotBeFoldedIsKeptNotDeleted`, FAILS on the real driver (sqlite-jdbc 3.46.1.3, JDK 21, also as a non-root user, so it is not the sandbox): when `lunavera.db` cannot be opened as a database, SQLite itself deletes the `lunavera.db-wal` file as soon as the connection fails (checked with plain JDBC, no app code), so `foldLeftoverLogInto` returns false but there is no `-wal` left to move to `lunavera.db-wal.unfolded-<time>`. The promise "the log is kept instead of deleted" is therefore not kept. Idea: copy the `-wal` file into `data/backups/` BEFORE the first connection is opened, and delete that copy only after the fold succeeded. Tick it when `aLogThatCannotBeFoldedIsKeptNotDeleted` passes.)* *(Session 150, DONE, run on real SQLite 3.46.1.3: `BackupService.foldLeftoverLogInto` copies the `-wal` file aside (`lunavera.db-wal.keep`) BEFORE the first connection, deletes the copy once the fold worked, and puts it back when the fold failed and SQLite had deleted the log, so `aLogThatCannotBeFoldedIsKeptNotDeleted` passes. Writing the end-to-end test showed a second hole the unit test could not: in a real restore the account comparison (`accountsBroughtBack`) opens the damaged live file again, and that second failed open deletes the log a second time. So `applyPendingRestore` now moves an unfoldable log into `data/backups/lunavera.db-wal.unfolded-<time>` (new `keepUnfoldedLog`) right after the failed fold, before anything else opens the file, and the side-file loop no longer has a special case. 2 new tests (a folded log leaves no copy behind; a whole restore over a damaged database ends with the log's exact bytes in the backups folder, no log beside the restored file, no `.keep` left); `BackupServiceTest` 38 tests green 3 times in a row; full SQLite suite 1,745 run, 6 fail (was 7), none new: the 5 of item 2.86 and `OrderNotesDaoTest` (item 2.100). **Not done:** the restore message still only names the safety zip; it does not tell the person that an unfoldable log was kept in the backups folder (needs a new text in both languages).)* *(DONE in code, Session 254, 2026-11-08: the restore message now says when an unfoldable log was kept. `BackupService.keepUnfoldedLog` returns whether it moved a log, the result text adds `backup.restored.unfolded` in both languages, and `UnfoldedLogNoteTest` (3 tests, no database) was run here and passes. The "Not done" sentence above is closed.)*

[x] **[Med] Paying (or autosaving) an open order from a stale screen deletes the lines another till added to it.** **Ticked 2026-11-07 (clean-up scan): the code, tests and messages this item names were found in the source by reading; nothing was run (no Maven, no JavaFX, no database), so the checks in `TODO.md` section 0 still apply.** **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** `OrderDAO.payExistingOrder` marks the order PAID and then runs `DELETE FROM order_items WHERE order_id = ?` and inserts the lines the till is showing; the autosave, `updateIfStillOpen`, makes the saved lines equal to the screen's (`OrderItemDiff` deletes every saved line that is not on screen). Neither compares with what the till loaded. With two tills on one MariaDB (4.14): a waiter adds a drink to table 5 at till B while the cashier at till A, whose screen was loaded earlier, takes the payment; the drink disappears from the order, so it is never charged, never taken off the stock and never in a report, and nobody sees a message. The risk is written down in section 1 ("two tills editing the same open order end with the last save") but only as a remark; the effect is on the money, so it needs its own fix. **(confirmed in code; never tried with two real tills)** Fix: a revision number on `orders` (`rev`, up by one whenever the lines, the discount or the customer change, in the same statements); the screen keeps the `rev` it loaded; autosave and payment use `WHERE id = ? AND status = OPEN AND rev = ?`, and on 0 rows with the order still open they stop with "Bàn này vừa được sửa ở máy khác" and reload the lines (offering to add back what the cashier put in on this till). Add the column the way `queue_no` was added (both schemas, `LATER_COLUMNS`, the importer, the schema-parity test). Tests with two connections: B adds a line, A's stale payment is refused and the order stays OPEN with both lines; A's stale autosave is refused; the normal one-till flow is unchanged and does not need a reload. *(Found independently by 2 scans; the texts are joined here.)* Also found by a second scan, with a cheaper first step: item 3.28 only covers an order that is no longer OPEN, not one that has changed (example: till 2 loaded table 5, the waiter on till 1 adds two cakes, and the next autosave or the payment on till 2 removes the cakes: the bill is short, the stock is not taken and nobody is told). If a revision column is too much for a first fix, remember the saved row ids that the screen loaded and never delete a row the screen did not see, then reload and tell the cashier. This is also what the "several tills at once" check in section 0 should try. *(DONE IN CODE 2026-11-02, left unticked until you have tried it with two real tills: **`orders.rev`** (`INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, both schemas, `LATER_COLUMNS`, the importer, `LaterColumnsExistTest`) goes up by one on every autosave (`OrderDAO.updateIfStillOpen`) and on a merge or a split (`TableMoveDAO`); the screen keeps the revision it loaded or last saved (`Order.rev`). The autosave and the payment (`payExistingOrder(..., expectedRev)`, called by `CheckoutService.pay` and, for the order a split comes from, `TableMoveDAO.keepRemainder`) run `WHERE ... AND rev = ?`; with 0 rows and the order still OPEN they throw the new `OrderChangedElsewhereException` (carrying the lines the order has now) and write nothing, with the order closed they answer as before (`false` / "no longer open", 3.28). The till catches it in both places (`StaleOrderAlert`): "Bàn này vừa được sửa ở máy khác" (`pos.stale.*`), the screen is loaded again from the saved order, and the message lists the lines this screen had that the order lacks (`StaleOrderRules.missingHere`) so the cashier can type them again; adding them back by a button was not built. The old payment signatures still work and skip the check. **Tests:** `OrderRevisionTest` (7, real SQLite, two copies of one order standing for two tills: the revision counts, a stale autosave is refused and keeps the other till's lines, a stale payment is refused with the order still OPEN and no stock taken, the reloaded till can pay, the one-till flow needs no reload, a closed order still answers the old way) and `StaleOrderRulesTest` (4) pass; switching the two checks off makes two of them fail. **Not tested:** the screen message, the split and merge paths with two connections, and MariaDB (the new column is in `MariaDbSchema`; `MariaDbSchemaTest` and the importer test pass). `OrderController` stays under its ceiling (1525 lines).)*

[x] **[Med] A power cut can take back the last paid sales: SQLite runs with `synchronous = NORMAL`.** **Ticked 2026-11-07 (clean-up scan): the code, tests and messages this item names were found in the source by reading; nothing was run (no Maven, no JavaFX, no database), so the checks in `TODO.md` section 0 still apply.** **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** `DatabaseConfig.configureSqlite` sends `PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL` on every connection once write-ahead logging is on, with the comment that "a power cut still cannot damage the file" (item 6.16 says the same). That is true for the file's structure, but not for the last sales: with WAL and `NORMAL`, SQLite flushes the log only at a checkpoint, so a payment that was committed (the cashier saw "paid", the cash is in the drawer) can be rolled back after a power loss or an operating-system crash; only a crash of the program itself is survived. Power cuts are common enough in small shops that this is worth closing, and the owner has no receipt printer (4.13), so no paper copy would show what was lost. **(from the SQLite documentation on WAL durability; a power cut cannot be staged here, so not reproduced)** Use `PRAGMA synchronous = FULL` (one extra flush per payment, not noticeable at a till's speed), change the comment and the 6.16 text, and say in `docs/DATABASE.md` that MariaDB must keep `innodb_flush_log_at_trx_commit = 1` (its default). Test: a new connection answers `PRAGMA synchronous` with 2 once WAL is active, and with 2 also when WAL could not be switched on. *(Session 151, 2.92 DONE IN CODE, left unticked until your `./mvnw -B clean test` is green. **Decision: FULL, always** (in WAL mode and when WAL is refused): `DatabaseConfig.configureSqlite` sends `PRAGMA synchronous = FULL` on every SQLite connection (constant `SQLITE_SYNCHRONOUS`), the `if (walActive)` and its comment are gone, and the comment now says what NORMAL did not promise. The other options were not taken: `EXTRA` adds a directory flush that only matters in rollback-journal mode, and keeping NORMAL with a periodic forced checkpoint would still lose the commits since the last one. **Cost, measured in real SQLite here on this machine's disk: 0.18 ms per commit for FULL against 0.01 ms for NORMAL (300 commits), i.e. one extra flush per payment or autosave; on a spinning disk it is a few milliseconds, which a till does not notice.** `docs/DATABASE.md` has a durability paragraph and says MariaDB must keep `innodb_flush_log_at_trx_commit = 1` (the code does not check that on a server; checking `@@innodb_flush_log_at_trx_commit` at start-up and warning would be a small follow-up). The 6.16 text in `COMPLETE.md` got a note. **Tests:** `SqliteDurabilitySourceTest` (4, no driver): the constant is FULL, `synchronous = NORMAL` appears nowhere, the setting is sent unconditionally, the docs name the MariaDB setting; ran and passed, and putting the old line back made it fail. `SqliteDurabilityTest` (2, real driver): a program connection answers 2 with WAL on, and a connection set up the way it is when WAL is refused (in-memory) also answers 2; it compiles but was NOT run (no SQLite driver here). A power cut itself cannot be staged, as the item says.)*

[x] **[Med] A refund of one line of an order paid with a gift card gave cash, and the card stayed as it was.** *(DONE in code, session 149; the database tests are written but NOT run, no SQLite driver here; the SQL and the design were run on real SQLite instead.)* The item said: `GiftCardDAO.restoreForOrder` only ran in a whole-order cancel, `RefundDAO.refund` never touched the card, and a card sold for 100.000 and an order of 60.000 paid with it, refunded by one line "in cash", left the card at 40.000 and took 60.000 out of the shift's expected cash. **Rule (mine; please confirm): the card gets its money back FIRST.** A line refund goes back onto the card(s) the order spent, up to what the order still has spent on them (the net of its `gift_card_moves`), and only the rest by the method the cashier chose; the result is the same as refunding the whole order, whatever order the lines are refunded in, and a card never gets more than the order took from it. **How:** `service/RefundSplit` (pure) turns the plan into rows, lines in order taking the card money first; `GiftCardDAO.restoreUpTo` (on the refund's own transaction, with a `gift_card_moves` line per card, in proportion to what each card has spent, `GiftCardRules.shares`) and `spentNet`; `restoreForOrder` now calls the same code. `RefundDAO.refund` writes one `refunds` row per piece of money: the card part with method GIFT_CARD, the rest with the chosen method; a line split between the two is two rows, the card row carrying the pieces and the line's share and the other row 0 of both, so the pieces are counted once, `RefundMath` and the line reports are unchanged, the shift's cash (only rows paid back in CASH) and the report by payment method (the row's method) needed NO change. A refund by method "gift card" is refused for more than the card paid (`refund.method.giftcard`). `RefundDAO.State` has `cardLeft` and `cardPart(amount)`, `Result` has `toCard`; the audit line and the security log say how much went back onto the card. **Dialog:** shows "of this X goes back onto the gift card, Y by the method chosen", switches the method box off when the card takes it all, adds a sentence to the confirmation and to the done message (4 new texts in both languages). I also fixed the box's starting method: it started as GIFT_CARD for an order paid by card although that method is not in its list; it now starts as cash. **Run here:** `RefundSplitTest` (9, no database) passes, with `GiftCardRules.shares` (never more than a card spent, no dong lost or invented in rounding); the real `SalesLedger` SQL on SQLite with a card payment and a part-card payment: the old single CASH row reproduces the bug (drawer 0 to -40.000), the new rows leave the drawer unchanged for a card payment and reduce it by only the 20.000 cash part for a part-card payment, the report by payment method shows GIFT_CARD 20.000 left and no cash, the product report counts one piece and the right money; the card queries (net spent per card, nothing left after the give-backs). `GiftCardTest` gets 9 tests (line refund of a card payment, of a part-card payment, two line refunds then the refused whole refund, a card method for more than it paid, restoreUpTo over two cards, nothing chosen, an order with no card) that compile but are **not run**. 410 no-database tests pass; the only failures are the ones that fail on the unchanged upload too. **Found on the way:** `order_payments` has `UNIQUE (order_id, method)`, so an order cannot be paid with two gift cards today (the payment screen only offers one); `restoreUpTo` handles several cards anyway and is tested directly. **Not done:** refunds made BEFORE this change on card orders stay as they were (recorded as cash, card never restored); the owner can find them as the orders with a spend in `gift_card_moves` and rows in `refunds` with a method other than GIFT_CARD, and top the card up by hand. The dialog was not seen on a screen.

[x] **[Med] On MariaDB (the default database) three "one at a time" rules are checked only by the code, not by the database.** **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** SQLite has them as unique indexes, MariaDB has none: `SchemaMigrations.upgrade` returns straight after `ensureMariaDataRules` for any database that is not SQLite, and `ensureOneOpenOrderPerTable` (a partial unique index, which MariaDB does not have) sits below that return. (1) One OPEN order per table (item 3.22): its own text says "Gap: MariaDB has no such index", the item is ticked in `COMPLETE.md` and no open item stands behind that gap, so two tills that open the same free table in the same instant can both succeed. (2) One open clock-in per person (Session 144): `TimeClockDAO.clockIn` reads `open(connection, userId)` and then inserts, and `time_clock` has no unique index on either database; on SQLite two writers are serialised so it is much less likely there *(inferred)*, on MariaDB nothing stops two inserts. (3) The take-away number of the day (item 4.25): `OrderDAO.nextQueueNumber` is `MAX(queue_no) + 1` read inside the insert transaction, and its own comment admits that two tills can get the same number. **(confirmed by reading the code; no database server was available to try it)** Fix: on MariaDB a generated column plus a unique key, for example `ALTER TABLE orders ADD COLUMN open_table_key INT GENERATED ALWAYS AS (CASE WHEN status = 'OPEN' THEN table_number END) VIRTUAL, ADD UNIQUE KEY ux_orders_one_open_per_table (open_table_key)` (a unique key accepts any number of NULLs; check the syntax against the oldest MariaDB/MySQL that `docs/DATABASE.md` supports), the same idea for `time_clock` (`user_id` while `clock_out IS NULL`), and for the take-away number a unique key on the day and the number with one retry on a duplicate; on SQLite a partial unique index on `time_clock(user_id) WHERE clock_out IS NULL`. Build them the way `buildUnique` does (skipped with a warning while old duplicates exist), turn the refusal into the clear messages that exist today (`order.table.hasopen`, `time.clock.already.in`), and run a test for each in the MariaDB job of `ci.yml`. Rare in a one-till shop; it matters as soon as a second till is added. *(Session 148, DONE in code and tests, run on a real SQLite and a real MariaDB 10.11: all three rules are now rules of the database on both. **SQLite:** new partial unique index `ux_time_clock_one_open` (one open clock-in per person) and unique index `ux_orders_queue_day_no`; the one-open-order index was already there. **MariaDB:** stored generated columns `orders.open_table_key` (the table number while the order is OPEN) and `time_clock.open_user_key` (the person while clocked in), each with a unique key, and a unique key on `orders.queue_day, queue_no`; new databases are created with them (`MariaDbSchema`), older ones get them at start-up (`SchemaMigrations.ensureMariaOneAtATimeKeys`, like the other unique rules: skipped with a warning while duplicates exist, built at a later start). New column `orders.queue_day` (the shop day of the take-away number, 4.50; added to both databases through `LATER_COLUMNS`, filled for older orders with their calendar day, copied by the SQLite-to-MariaDB importer). The take-away number is now read from `queue_day`; when the database refuses a number another till took at the same moment (or stops the save as a deadlock loser) the whole save is run again, up to 8 times, in a new transaction. `TimeClockDAO.clockIn` turns a refused second clock-in into the same clear message as before; the order insert already did that for a table. 14 new tests (`OneAtATimeRulesTest` 10, `OneAtATimeKeysTest` 4), three of them true races of several threads, green 3 times in a row on SQLite and on MariaDB. Whole suite on MariaDB: 1,688 run, 29 fail, the same 29 as on the untouched project on the same server; on SQLite 1,743 run and the same 18 as before plus one importer-column test that was then fixed and passes. **Not done / notes:** an import of a SQLite file that itself holds duplicates (two open orders on a table, two open clock-ins) is now refused by MariaDB's keys; moving an open order onto a table that got an order in the same instant (`TableMoveDAO`) is stopped by the key but shows the generic message; the retry loop in `OrderDAO.insert` is not re-indented.)*

[x] **[Low] A customer's "total spent" ignores line refunds, and the customer screen reads every paid order each time.** **Ticked 2026-11-07 (clean-up scan): the code, tests and messages this item names were found in the source by reading; nothing was run (no Maven, no JavaFX, no database), so the checks in `TODO.md` section 0 still apply.** **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** `CustomerDAO.paidOrderRows` selects every PAID order that has a customer (no `GROUP BY`, no date limit) and `CustomerStats` adds up `orders.total`. Line refunds (the `refunds` table) are not taken off, so a customer who had a drink refunded still shows the full amount, while the shift close, the reports and the dashboard net refunds through `SalesLedger`. The whole result is also loaded into memory on every open of the customer screen, which grows with each year of sales. Needed: take the refunds off and let the database do the sums (`GROUP BY customer_id`). *(Session 154, 2.101 DONE IN CODE, left unticked until your `./mvnw -B clean test` is green. `CustomerDAO.paidOrderRows` and `CustomerStats.aggregate` / `Row` are gone: **`CustomerDAO.figures()`** runs ONE grouped statement (`GROUP BY customer_id`, only PAID orders with a customer) and returns one row per customer: visits, `SUM(total)`, the line refunds, and the last visit. The refunds are summed per order first and joined (`LEFT JOIN (SELECT order_id, SUM(amount) ... GROUP BY order_id)`), so an order with two refunded lines is not counted twice. **Total spent = paid orders less line refunds** (`CustomerStats.figures`, never below zero), the same netting `SalesLedger` gives the reports; the visit still counts because the order stands (as `SalesLedger` counts it), and an order refunded whole is no longer PAID and is not counted, as before. Last visit: the latest readable time; a time that cannot be read is left out of it only. The pattern accepts SQLite's `T` and MariaDB's space, and avoids `||`, which MariaDB reads as OR. The customer screen and the CSV export use `figures()` (two lines in `CustomerController`, no line count changed). **Run here:** the exact statement in real SQLite against the project's table definitions (8 checks: refunds taken off, two refund rows on one order, a refunded-whole order excluded, no-customer orders excluded, an unreadable time ignored for last visit, a space-separated time read) all passed; `CustomerStatsTest` rewritten for the new API (12) passed, and ignoring refunds failed 4 of them. Two new database tests in `Section4ReportsTest` (a partial and a full line refund; one row per customer over four orders) and the old purchase-figures test moved to `figures()`: compile, NOT run (no SQLite driver). The MariaDB statement was not run. Not done: showing refunds next to the total on the customer screen, and a cached total.)* (Name check 2026-11-12: `CustomerDAO.paidOrderRows` and `CustomerStats.aggregate` are gone; now `CustomerDAO.figures()` and `CustomerStats.figures(...)`.)

## 2A. Security flaws  **NEW (4th scan)**

This is a local desktop app with one till, so the main risks are a curious or careless person at the till, a lost computer or USB stick, and mistakes that lock the shop out. Related items found earlier:


bug 2.2 (default admin password stays usable), bug 2.3 (unlimited discounts), bug 2.12 (password hash strength), edge case 3.8 (any employee can empty a table) and feature 4.2 (audit log).

1. [x] **[Med] CSV export can run a formula in Excel.** `ReportWindow.csv()` only quotes commas, quotes and line breaks. A product named `=HYPERLINK("http://...")` or starting with `+`, `-` or `@` becomes a live formula when the report is opened in Excel. **DONE in code, session 43:** CSV cells that start with `= + - @`, a tab or a carriage return get a leading apostrophe (`Csv.cell`; used by the report export; tested).
       Prefix such cells with an apostrophe or a space (and treat a leading tab or carriage return the same way). Add a test. **(confirmed in code)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** `csv()` returned `=HYPERLINK(...)`, `+1+1`, `@SUM(A1)` and `-2+3` unchanged (only the first got quotes, from its inner double quotes); a value with a bare carriage return (`a\rb`) is not quoted either.
2. [x] **[Med] The "last admin" rule can be beaten.** The check (`countActiveAdmins() <= 1`) lives in the screen, then `UserDAO.updateStatus` / `updateRole` run as a separate step with no guard. Two admins working at once can lock or demote each other and leave no active admin, *(DONE in code, session 42: the rule is one UPDATE inside `AccountService`, checked by the database in the same statement; tested with the last admin.)*
       and the only way back in is editing the database by hand. Enforce it in the DAO in one transaction (for example `UPDATE ... WHERE (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM users WHERE role='ADMIN' AND status='ACTIVE') > 1`) and test it. **(confirmed in code)**

Two questions were still open then; both are closed further down (employees and the accounts list: 13th pass; the checks inside the DAOs: session 79). *(8th pass, by reading the callers: account creation, editing and locking, restore and refund are enforced below the screen already. `UserDAO.updateStatus` and `updateRole` have no caller in the app at all (the account service writes its own SQL); `updatePasswordHash` is called only by the person's own password change and the login re-hash. Product and category writes are called only from `ProductController`, `CategoryController` and `CategoryDialog` (one `AdminController` before the 13th-pass split), which send a non-admin away in `initialize()` through `AdminScreen.blockNonAdmin`, and since the 13th pass the delete flows also check the permission in `CategoryService` and `ProductService` (so the old question about employees seeing the accounts list is closed: they cannot open it).        *(Original advice, kept for history; both parts are settled: the permission check now sits in the service/DAO layer, and employees cannot open the accounts list at all, see the 13th-pass note above.)* **(confirmed)**
       **DONE in code (session 79, by reading and by running the real classes; no database here):** `ProductDAO.insert/update/delete/setActive` and `CategoryDAO.insert/update/deleteIfUnused` now call `Permissions.requireAdmin` first, so a cashier, or nobody, is refused below the screen as with accounts, restore and refund. 13. [x] **[Low] A stored password hash can make the login hang.** **NEW (5th scan)** `PasswordUtil` reads the iteration count from the stored value and uses it as is. A damaged or hand-edited hash with a huge number (for example `99999999`) keeps a login thread busy for minutes. **DONE in code, session 43:** a stored iteration count outside 1,000 to 5,000,000 is refused as a failed login at once and logged (tested: 99,999,999 and 0 are refused in well under 2 s).
        Anyone who can edit the database can do worse, so this is hygiene: when verifying, reject a count outside a sane range (say 10,000 to 5,000,000) as a failed login and log it. Add a test. **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** a stored count of 20,000,000 made one login take **21 s** (the normal 120,000 takes 0.15 s here), so 99,999,999 would be about 105 s. Counts of 0, -1 and 1 are rejected as a failed login, so my first note about "accepted" was wrong.
