[x] **[Med] A MariaDB shop with a second till sends everything over the network unencrypted, and a hung server freezes the till.** **NEW (8th scan)** `DatabaseSettings.jdbcUrl` builds `jdbc:mariadb://host:port/db?connectTimeout=5000` and nothing else: there is no way to ask for TLS (`sslMode`) and no `socketTimeout`. `docs/DATABASE.md` section 7 allows other tills to connect, and then customer names, phone numbers, password hashes and the database password itself cross the shop's wifi in clear. Without a read timeout, a server that stops answering (power cut at the router, a stuck query) blocks the call forever, and many calls run on the screen thread (see 6.14), so the window freezes with no message. **(confirmed in code)** Fix: optional `db.ssl=true` / `db.options=` in `database.properties`, `socketTimeout` of about 30 s, and say in the docs that a remote server needs TLS. *(DONE in code, session 83: `DatabaseSettings` has new keys. `db.ssl=true` means TLS with the server's certificate and name checked (`sslMode=verify-full`); `db.sslMode=` (disable, trust, verify-ca or verify-full) chooses exactly and wins over `db.ssl`, and `db.ssl=true` with `db.sslMode=disable` is refused; `db.sslServerCert=` is the certificate or CA file of a self-signed server (it must exist when the mode checks certificates, a name without a folder is read from the data folder, it goes into the URL as `serverSslCert=` with forward slashes and the characters `space % & = # ?` escaped); `db.socketTimeoutMs=` (default 30000, 0 = wait for ever, otherwise 1000 to 3,600,000). The URL is now `jdbc:mariadb://host:port/db?connectTimeout=5000&socketTimeout=30000&sslMode=disable` (or the chosen mode). A server on another computer with nothing encrypting the connection gets a WARNING in the log at start-up (`insecureRemoteWarning`, no password in it) and the line on the login screen says "KHÔNG mã hóa" ("mã hóa TLS" when it is encrypted, "chưa kiểm tra chứng chỉ" for `trust`); a server on this computer says nothing. An IPv6 host is now put in square brackets in the URL. SQLite ignores all of it. Docs: `docs/DATABASE.md` sections 3 and 7 (server side, `REQUIRE SSL`, which mode to pick, the timeout) and `database.properties.example`. Tests: 13 new in `DatabaseSettingsTest`, 2 in `LocationSummaryTest`; compiled and run here with stand-in libraries. Not tried: a real MariaDB with TLS (section 0); the URL parameter names `sslMode`, `serverSslCert` and `socketTimeout` are those of MariaDB Connector/J 3.x as documented, the pom has 3.4.1. Not covered: the `mysqldump` backup, which does not read these keys (2A.42).)* *(Name check 2026-11-08: `DatabaseSettings.jdbcUrl` is now `DatabaseSettings.mariaDbUrl()`, which also sets the timeout and the TLS options (`SslMode`).)*

[x] **[Low] Typing 1,000 different user names wipes the login delay of every account, "admin" included.** **NEW (10th scan)** `LoginThrottle.recordFailure` does `states.clear()` when `MAX_TRACKED` (1000) names are tracked, and the comment says an attacker "only resets everyone's counters". Each wrong attempt costs the attacker one password hash (a fraction of a second), so a script or a keyboard-emulating USB stick needs only minutes to reset the delay and then get two free tries again. It is a speed bump rather than a lock, so this is Low, but the cap that protects memory should not also erase the protection. **(confirmed in code)** When the map is full, drop the expired entries first, then the oldest ones, never all; test with 1,001 names that the entry for "admin" survives. Related: 2A.19, 2A.27, 2A.28. *(DONE in code, session 84: `LoginThrottle.recordFailure` no longer clears the table. When a NEW name arrives and 1,000 are tracked, `makeRoom` first drops every entry that has been quiet for 15 minutes (it would be forgotten anyway), and if that frees nothing it drops the weakest one: a name that is not waiting before one that is, then fewer wrong attempts, then the oldest. Names typed in a flood have one attempt and no wait, so they push each other out and not the name that has a delay running; a name already in the table is never pushed out by its own next failure (the old code cleared everything even then). Tests in `LoginThrottleTest`: 3,000 other names leave admin's delay untouched, a full table loses exactly one entry, a name in a full table keeps counting, fewer-failures-first, everyone-waiting, quiet-first. What remains: a flood can still cost a name that has only its two free attempts (it falls back to 0 of 2), which gives an attacker at most two extra tries per name per flood; the delay is still kept in memory only (2A.19).)*

[x] **[Med] The transfer-QR integrity record lives in the same folder as the picture, so whoever can swap the picture can also reset the check.** **NEW (11th scan)** `TransferQrGuard.inspect` compares the picture with `qr-transfer.sha256` next to it; if that file is missing it records the current picture and returns `FIRST_SEEN`, which is trusted silently (the only callers treat everything except `CHANGED` as fine), and any `IOException` also returns `OK`. So the attack that 2A.17 was made to stop (put your own account's QR in `data/`) only needs one more step: delete `qr-transfer.sha256` (or write the new picture's hash into it), and the next start accepts the new picture without a warning, a log line apart. An older shop's `qr-transfer.sha256` is taken over once, only when it still matches the picture, and then deleted, so an upgrade does not ask for a re-confirmation. The payment dialog and the admin dashboard show a message for each state. Limits: on a SQLite shop the database file sits in the same data folder, so this raises the bar (editing a database instead of deleting a file) rather than closing it; on a MariaDB shop the record is on the server. Tests: rewritten `TransferQrGuardTest` (12, with an in-memory store, the old attack included) and `TransferQrGuardDatabaseTest` (2, not run here: no SQLite driver in this sandbox).)*

[x] **[Low] The restore preview has no size limit, so a damaged or crafted backup can fill the disk before the restore's own limits apply.** **NEW (24th scan)** `BackupService.inspectInto` (used by the dialog's preview and by `scheduleRestore`) copies the zip entry `lunavera.db` into a temp file with a plain `Files.copy` and reads every other entry to its end; the 1 GiB, 50 MiB and 2 GiB caps (`MAX_DATABASE_BYTES`, `MAX_PICTURE_BYTES`, `MAX_PICTURES_TOTAL_BYTES`) are used only later, in the step that applies the restore (`copyCapped`). A zip whose `lunavera.db` expands to tens of gigabytes (a small file of repeated bytes) fills the disk under `data/tmp` while the person only wanted to *look* at it, and the till then fails every sale with a disk-full error (2.78) until the temp file is removed at the next start. **(confirmed in code; not tried with a bomb)** Use `copyCapped` for the database entry and count the bytes of the other entries against the same caps inside `inspectInto`; test with a generated zip that expands past the limit. *(DONE in code, Session 85 (also closes 2A.30): `inspectInto` copies the database entry with `copyCapped` (1 GiB), reads every other entry through a capped read (50 MiB each, 2 GiB for the pictures together) so the CRC is still checked, reads `backup-info.txt` up to 16 KiB only, and a plain `.db` file over the cap is refused before it is copied. The caps are a `Limits` parameter (`inspect(Path, Limits)`) so the test uses small ones. A refused look leaves no partial file in `data/tmp`. Test: `lookingAtABackupKeepsToTheSizeLimits` (needs SQLite, not run here). Not tried with a real multi-gigabyte zip.)*

Unicode line and paragraph separators (U+2028, U+2029, U+0085), the vertical tab and form feed (U+000B, U+000C) and the ESC character (U+001B) pass through; some log viewers and editors start a new line at them, and a terminal that shows the log (`tail`) acts on ESC sequences. Anyone can type them into the user-name box (2A.39 is the same box). **(confirmed in code; a viewer that splits on U+2028 is needed to see the effect)** Replace every character that is an ISO control character or in the Unicode categories `Zl`/`Zp` with a space, keep the 40-character cut, and add the cases to a `SecurityLogTest`. *(DONE in code, Session 86: every control character, U+0080-U+009F (U+0085), U+2028 and U+2029 becomes a space; the 40-character cut stays. Test: `cleanReplacesEveryLineSeparatorAndControlCharacter` in `SecurityLogRedactTest`; run here: passed.)*

[x] **[Low] The backup button's `mysqldump` ignores the TLS settings.** **NEW (session 83)** `db.ssl`, `db.sslMode` and `db.sslServerCert` (2A.26) are used for the JDBC connection only. `MariaDbBackup` starts `mysqldump` with host, port, user and database, and the password in `MYSQL_PWD`, and nothing about TLS, so against a server that requires it (`REQUIRE SSL`, `require_secure_transport`) the backup button fails, and against one that does not require it the dump crosses the network in the clear even though the till's own queries are encrypted. `docs/DATABASE.md` section 7 tells the owner to put `ssl-ca=` under `[client]` in `my.ini`. **(confirmed in code; the `[client]` advice was not tried against a real server)** Pass the options to `mysqldump` too, but the two clients disagree (MariaDB's `--ssl`, `--ssl-verify-server-cert`, `--ssl-ca`; MySQL 8's `--ssl-mode=REQUIRED|VERIFY_CA|VERIFY_IDENTITY`), so first find out which one `db.mysqldump` points to (`mysqldump --version`, the same probe as 2.74) and build the arguments for that one. Test the argument builder with both version strings. *(DONE in code, Session 108: `MariaDbBackup` now passes the connection's TLS settings to `mysqldump`. When `db.ssl`/`db.sslMode` ask for encryption it first runs `mysqldump --version` (a few seconds at most; not run at all for an unencrypted connection, so nothing changes there) and builds the options of that family: MySQL gets `--ssl-mode=REQUIRED|VERIFY_CA|VERIFY_IDENTITY` and `--ssl-ca=<db.sslServerCert>`; MariaDB gets `--ssl`, `--ssl-verify-server-cert` and `--ssl-ca=`. MariaDB's program cannot check the CA without also checking the host name, so with `verify-ca` its backup is a little stricter than the connection (fails with an error, never dumps in the clear); `trust` on a new MariaDB client (11.4+, which verifies by default) may also be refused. If the program says neither "MariaDB" nor "MySQL" and TLS is wanted, **no backup is started** and the message (`db.dump.flavour.unknown`, both languages) tells the owner to write the full path of the server's own `mysqldump` in `db.mysqldump`. `docs/DATABASE.md` section 7 rewritten (the `[client]` advice is no longer needed). Tests: 10 new in `MariaDbBackupTest` (the two families told apart, every mode for both families, the CA file, refusal when unknown, password never on the command line, and end-to-end with a stand-in `mysqldump` script that answers `--version`); all 19 tests of the class passed here. **Not tried against a real server**, MariaDB or MySQL: the option spellings come from the clients' documentation.)*

With the language switch (1.7) the app's own texts can be English, and they have no Vietnamese letters. In the merged code `ErrorText.forScreen` therefore falls back to the old deny-list (`TECHNICAL` and `DRIVER_TEXT`) when the interface is not Vietnamese, so an English driver text that the lists do not know ("Read-only file system") is shown there, and any new English text of the app that happens to match a pattern ("does not exist", "not found", "Cannot ...") is hidden: `I18nTest.noMessageLooksLikeATechnicalErrorToErrorText` fails the build when that happens, which is how `csv.p.nocategory`, `startup.nosettings` and `backup.err.notwritable` had to be reworded. **(confirmed by running `ErrorTextTest`, two tests added for both interfaces)** The real fix is a marker: an exception type `UserMessage` (or a `DataAccessException` flag) for texts that come from `I18n`, and `forScreen` shows exactly those; everything else is logged and replaced by `generic()` in either language. Tests: the technical texts of `ErrorTextTest` in both interfaces. Related: 2A.38, 1.7, 5.15. *(DONE in code, found by the 2026-10-24 scan: the merged tree does not have the deny-list fallback this item describes (that was the older branch `_5_`). `ErrorText.isOwnMessage` shows a text when it has no technical pattern and either has Vietnamese letters or matches one of the texts in the language files of all languages (`I18n.LANGUAGES`, `bundleFor`, `keys`, `{n}` placeholders matching any text); everything else, in either interface, is logged and replaced by `generic()`. `ErrorTextTest.theAppsOwnEnglishTextsAreShownInEnglishAndDriverTextIsStillHidden` and `everyTextOfBothLanguageFilesThatIsAnErrorMessageIsRecognised` cover it, and `I18nTest.noMessageLooksLikeATechnicalErrorToErrorText` still guards the texts that match a technical pattern. The marker type `UserMessage` proposed above was not needed. Not run: Maven.)* *(Name check 2026-11-08: same as 2A.38: the marker type `UserMessage` was not built; `util/ErrorText` does the job.)* *(Name check 2026-11-08: the key `backup.err.notwritable` was reworded to `backup.dir.notwritable`.)*

[x] **[Med] Expenses and vouchers are missing from the audit log, and deleting an expense leaves no amount behind.** **DONE in code (Session 231, 2026-11-07); the database test and the transfer-QR store were not run here (no SQLite driver), see "Session 231" in `progress.md`.** Six new actions in `AuditAction`: `EXPENSE_RECORDED` (also for the cost a delivery of ingredients writes), `EXPENSE_DELETED` (old value = the amount; the note holds the day, whether it came from the drawer, and the note, so the figures survive the row), `VOUCHER_CREATED` (value and limits), `VOUCHER_SWITCHED` (on/off, only when the state really changes), `TRANSFER_QR_CHANGED` (picture chosen again, text only, or accepted; the old and new account text and the start of the picture's fingerprint) and `SETTINGS_CHANGED` (shop name, address, phone, tax code, low-stock level, day start; the rules already had `RULE_CHANGED`). Every line is written on the connection of the change, so a failed change leaves no line and a change never lacks one (the QR line goes in with the fingerprint save). New targets `EXPENSE` and `VOUCHER`, a model class `AuditSetting` for the setting names, texts in both languages. Not audited: the price note. Original text: **[Med] Expenses and vouchers are missing from the audit log, and deleting an expense leaves no amount behind.** **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** `AuditAction` has 32 actions (discounts, refunds, prices, stock, accounts, shifts, clock corrections, gift cards) but none for money leaving the drawer or for the codes that give discounts. `ExpenseDAO.record`, `ExpenseDAO.delete` and `VoucherDAO` (create, switch on or off) write only a free-text line through `SecurityLog.event`, which lives in a rotating text file and not in the database. `ExpenseDAO.delete` is a physical `DELETE FROM expenses` and its line says only "deleted expense 17": no amount, category, note or day. So an administrator can remove a cash-out and the only trace has no figures; a closed shift keeps its stored `cash_out` total but nothing says which lines it came from. **(confirmed: neither `ExpenseDAO` nor `VoucherDAO` calls `AuditDAO`)** Add `EXPENSE_RECORDED`, `EXPENSE_DELETED` (old value: day, amount, category, note), `VOUCHER_CREATED` and `VOUCHER_SWITCHED`, show them in the audit window like the others, and check whether the transfer-QR change (item 2A.17) is in the audit log or only in the text file. The existing test that every `AuditAction` has a text in both languages then guards the new ones. *(Found independently by 2 scans; the texts are joined here.)* A second scan adds two more things that leave no audit line: saving the shop settings (`SettingsDialog`) and accepting or replacing the transfer QR (`TransferQrGuard`, item 2A.17). They write only to the plain-text security log, which is rotated and can be edited on the PC, while the audit log lives in the database and is the owner's screen; a changed bank QR sends payments elsewhere. Suggested entries: `EXPENSE_ADDED`, `EXPENSE_DELETED`, `VOUCHER_SAVED`, `SETTINGS_CHANGED`, `TRANSFER_QR_CHANGED`, each with who, the amount and the old and new value where there is one.

A text that cannot be one of our codes counts for nothing. Tests: `GiftCardLookupGuardTest` (5), `AuditTextTest` +1, `GiftCardTest` +2 (database, not run here). **Original text:** **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** `GiftCardDAO.findByCode` (the "Kiểm tra" button in `SplitPaymentPanel` and the balance check in `GiftCardDialog`) answers for any code, any number of times, and a card is a bearer instrument: whoever knows `GC-XXXX-XXXX` can spend it. The code space is about 31^8 (8.5e11), so guessing by hand is not realistic, but nothing counts failed lookups or writes them to the audit log, unlike failed logins (`LoginThrottle`) and PIN tries (`PinService`). Reuse the same throttle for wrong codes (for example 5 wrong codes in a minute, then a short pause on that till) and write one audit line when it trips. Test: six wrong codes in a row, the sixth answers "wait" without touching the database. **(reasoned from the code; no abuse seen)**

Original text: **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** `ValidationUtil.isValidPassword` refuses a list of 25 passwords, so these pass: `letmein123`, `iloveyou123`, `qwertyuiop1`, `1q2w3e4r5t`, `trustno1x`, `caphe2026`, `admin2026!` and `aaaaaaaa1` (each one has 8 or more characters, a letter and a digit, so the rule lets it through). `PinRules.isAcceptable` refuses only all-the-same, going up and going down, so `121212`, `123123`, `112233`, `000111`, `696969` and `520520` pass. **(confirmed by running both checks)** A PIN is switched off after 5 wrong tries (`MAX_WRONG_IN_A_ROW`) and a password is slowed by the login delay, so the harm is limited; the account that matters is the administrator's. Keep a list of the commonest few thousand passwords as a resource file (and refuse a password made of one repeated character or a keyboard run), and add repeated pairs and triples (`121212`, `123123`) and a handful of common PINs to the easy list; add the examples above to the tests.

## 3. Niche and edge cases (most important first)
1. [x] **Ticked 2026-11-07 (clean-up scan): the code, tests and messages this item names were found in the source by reading; nothing was run (no Maven, no JavaFX, no database), so the checks in `TODO.md` section 0 still apply.** **Stock is only taken at payment.** Two open tables can both hold the last item and the second payment fails at the end; the product list on screen is
       also loaded once and can be out of date. Consider reserving stock when a line is added, or re-checking stock before the payment dialog. **(confirmed)** *(Session 148, 3.1 DONE IN CODE, left unticked until your `./mvnw -B clean test` is green and you have tried two tills on the last piece. **Design decision (made here, because the owner's D9 answer is not in the project files; please confirm it): a line in an OPEN order reserves what it needs, and the reservation is derived from the saved lines, never stored.** What an order may still sell is the shelf less what the OTHER open orders (tables and take-aways) hold; paying, cancelling or emptying a table frees it by itself, so there is no counter to drift and NO schema change. For a recipe product it works through the ingredients, so a latte and a cappuccino on the same milk compete. Paying is unchanged (the real stock is still taken, guarded, at payment; the earlier re-check before the payment box stays). **What changed:** `ProductDAO.findAllForSale(orderId)` (the till's product list, stock already net of the other orders' holds, `Product.heldElsewhere` says how much); the till reads it for the order on screen and again whenever that order changes; the refusal sentence now says when other orders hold part of the stock (`pos.stock.short.held`); `OrderDAO.updateGuarded` / `OrderService.autosave` check again INSIDE the autosave transaction, refuse a line that grows past what is free, write nothing and name the products, and the till then says so once (`HoldNotice`) and shows the order as saved; shrinking a line is never blocked. Pure rules in `service/StockHolds`; `StockCheck.message` took over the payment-box notice from the screen. `OrderController` is 1,531 lines (ceiling set to 1531; it was 1,536). The old `updateIfStillOpen` is unchanged and unguarded. **Honest limits:** exact on SQLite (one writer at a time); on MariaDB the product rows are locked first so two tills adding the same product are serialised, but two different drinks that share an ingredient can still pass together, and the payment guard is then the last word. An open order that nobody closes keeps its pieces held until it is paid, cancelled or the table emptied (the message says another order holds them; there is no time-out). Booking (table reservations) do not hold stock. **Verified here:** the exact SQL the DAO sends was run in real SQLite against the project's own table definitions: 23 scenarios (own lines not counted against the order, other orders add up, paid and cancelled release, never negative, take-aways hold, recipe servings through shared ingredients, scarcest ingredient decides, the IN list, the lock statement) all passed. `StockHoldsTest` (14, pure): ran and passed; three deliberate breaks each failed it. `StockHoldsDbTest` (8, real database) compiles but was NOT run (no SQLite driver). The screen was never shown; `javac` finds the same unresolved symbols in `OrderController` as before (JavaFX missing) and nothing new, and a wrong call in the new lines is reported. Not done: showing "held by table N" on the tiles, and a time-out for forgotten open orders.)* *(8th pass: not done: needs a design decision (reserve at add, or re-check before the payment dialog) and changes the POS screen, which cannot be loaded here.)* **Small version DONE in code, Session 132 (second task), not run and not seen on a screen; the item stays open for the big version:** pressing "Thanh toán" now first re-reads the products of the cart in one query (`ProductDAO.findByIds`, `service/StockCheck`) while the screen is locked as during a payment (`setPaymentInFlight`, so a second click cannot open two boxes), and only opens the payment box when everything is covered. Otherwise a message names each item (`"Latte: đơn có 4, chỉ còn 3."`, or `"...: đã ngừng bán."` for a product switched off or deleted meanwhile), says nothing has been charged, and the product tiles are refreshed; the cashier lowers the quantity or removes the line and presses pay again. The lines of one product are added up (as the payment does). It is a warning, not a guarantee: another till can still sell the item between the check and the click on "Xác nhận", and then the payment's own guarded `UPDATE ... stock >= ?` still fails cleanly (unchanged). If the re-read itself fails the box opens anyway. **Not done (needs decision D9, which is not in the project files):** reserving stock when a line is added, so two tables cannot both hold the last item. Tests: `StockCheckTest` 9, `StockRecheckDbTest` 3.
2. [x] **Editing a product can overwrite sales.** **NEW (2nd scan)** `ProductDAO.update` writes `stock = ?` with the number that was in the edit dialog. If a sale (another window, or a payment while the dialog is open)
       changes the stock after the dialog opened, saving the dialog puts the old number back and the sold items reappear. Fix: save stock changes as a difference
       (`stock = stock + ?`) or compare with the stock read when the dialog opened, and warn on a mismatch. **(confirmed)** **Reproduced in the 6th scan:** stock 10, a sale of 3 (stock 7), then saving a product object loaded before the sale (only the name changed) put the stock back to **10**. **DONE in code, 8th pass:** `Product` remembers the stock it was read with (`loadedStock`); `ProductDAO.update` writes `stock=COALESCE(?, stock)` and sends NULL when the stock number was not changed, so a rename or price edit no longer puts the old stock back over sales made while the dialog was open (the 6th-scan case: 10, sale of 3, rename: now stays 7). A stock number that was typed in is still written as typed (a stock-take); a sale made while the dialog was open is then not added back, which is what typing a counted number means. A product object that was not read from the database (new, or built in code) is still written in full. Checked: the new SQL on real SQLite (rename-only keeps 7, typed 20 gives 20, typed 0 gives 0); the changed files compile with the JDK compiler (0 errors); two tests added in `ProductDAOTest` (compile-checked, **not run**: no database driver here, so they need your `mvn test`).

[x] **[Med] Vietnamese letters are case-sensitive in the order-history search.** **NEW (7th scan)** The search is SQL `LIKE`, which folds case only for ASCII letters, so `nguyễn` does not find `Nguyễn` and `đào` does not find `Đào` in employee and customer names. Item 5.14 covers the controllers' `toLowerCase().contains` searches, not this SQL one. Keep a search key (NFD, no accents, lower case) or filter in Java after narrowing by date and status; add a test with `Đào`/`đào`. **(confirmed from the SQL; SQLite's ASCII-only LIKE folding is its documented behaviour)** *(13th pass: DONE in code. `DatabaseConfig` registers a SQLite function `VN_KEY(text)` on every connection (it calls `TextNormalizer.searchKey`: lower case, no accents, \"đ\" as \"d\"; NULL becomes an empty text). The history search now compares `VN_KEY(name)` with the typed text folded the same way, through `DatabaseConfig.searchKeySql(column)` (SQLite: `VN_KEY(column)`; MariaDB: the column itself, because its `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` collation already ignores case and accents). So `nguyễn`, `NGUYỄN` and `nguyen` all find `Nguyễn`, and `dao` finds `Đào`. New `VietnameseSearchTest` (5; the first four also run on MariaDB). The folding rule was checked in Python. NOT run: Maven, the tests. **Compile risk to look at first:** the registration uses `org.sqlite.Function.create(connection, name, function)` with `xFunc()`, `value_text(0)` and `result(String)`, written from memory of the sqlite-jdbc API and compiled here only against my own stand-in; if `mvn compile` complains, the fix is in `DatabaseConfig.registerSearchKeyFunction`. The name is searched with `LIKE` on the folded text, so a search is a little slower on a very large history (the function runs per row).)* *(Session 60, by `progress.md`: the data layer was compiled against the real `sqlite-jdbc` 3.46.1.3 and `VietnameseSearchTest` is not among the failures of that run, so the compile risk named above did not show up; Maven itself was still not run.)*

Used by `findTodayOrders`, `countToday`, `sumRevenueToday`, `countDistinctCustomersToday`, `revenueForLast7Days`, the history `FilterSql` (from and/or to, with the `o.` prefix) and every query of `ReportDAO`; `OrderDAO.EFFECTIVE_DAY` stays for what is SHOWN and GROUPED BY. SQLite had no index on `paid_at` (MariaDB already had `idx_orders_paid_at`), so schema version 3 adds it: `SchemaMigrations.createPaidAtIndex` (SQLite step 3, also for a fresh file) and `ensureMariaPaidAtIndex` (MariaDB step 3, registered in `MariaDbMigrations` itself because that class runs first at start-up). **Measured, which this item said was never done** (Python `sqlite3`, 300 000 orders, 3 % with no `paid_at`): one day, old `DATE(...)` rule 42 ms (`SCAN orders`) against 26 ms for the range form without the new index (still a scan) and 4.8 ms with it (`MULTI-INDEX OR` on `idx_orders_paid_at`); seven days 46 ms against 6 ms. **Same orders as before:** the exact SQL strings of `EffectiveDay` (printed from the compiled class) were run against the old `DATE(COALESCE(...))` rule on the awkward cases (opened 23:50 paid 00:10, paid at 00:00 sharp, 23:59:59.999999999, a date with no time, the MariaDB text with a space, never paid) and on 3000 random orders with 900 random closed and open-ended ranges: 0 differences. [x] **[Med] The profit figures of the report are wrong for discounted orders, and old days change when a cost is edited.** **NEW (12th scan)** Item 4.20 records both limits inside a ticked note but nothing is open. `ReportDAO.profit`, `topProducts` and `dailySales` compute `line_total - quantity * products.cost` per line: (1) the order's discount is not subtracted, while "revenue" is the order total after the discount, so for a discounted order the report says revenue minus costs is less than the profit it prints; with a 20 % staff discount on a shop's busy day the "Lợi nhuận gộp" can be well above the truth; (2) the cost is read from the product row *now*, so raising a cost today lowers the profit of last month, and lines of a product whose cost was empty then and filled in later start to count. **(confirmed in code)** Store the cost on the order line when the order is paid (`order_items.unit_cost`, a `LATER_COLUMNS` entry like 2A.18's, in both schemas and the importer, copied from `products.cost` in `OrderDAO`), compute profit from it, and spread the order discount over its lines in proportion to `line_total` (or subtract the order's discount once per order). Keep "-" for lines with no cost known at the time. Tests: a discounted order whose revenue minus cost equals the printed profit; a cost edit that does not move an earlier day. *(14th pass: DONE in code. New column `order_items.unit_cost` (`SqliteSchema.LATER_COLUMNS`, `MariaDbSchema`, the importer's column list), set by `OrderDAO.stampUnitCosts` inside the payment's own transaction (both `insert` of a paid order and `payExistingOrder`) from `products.cost`; null means not known then. `ReportDAO.profit`, `topProducts` and `dailySales` now use it and subtract each line's share of the order discount (`discount x line_total / subtotal`, summed and rounded once), so revenue minus costs equals the printed profit and a later cost edit does not move an earlier day; lines with no cost then stay \"not known\" even if a cost is entered later. A one-time job (`DatabaseConfig.backfillUnitCostOnce`, flag `order_items_unit_cost_v1`, after the import) gives the lines of orders paid or refunded before the upgrade their product's cost today, so the history does not go blank; open orders and products with no cost stay null, and it never runs again. The SQL was run on real SQLite with the project's own table definitions (discounted order 47,000 = 72,000 - 25,000; a cost edit leaves the day unchanged; the no-cost line is excluded; the backfill touches only paid and refunded lines). 4 new `Section4ReportsTest` cases and `UnitCostBackfillTest` (2), compiled, NOT run (need the driver). **Notes:** the profit of the days before the upgrade is only as good as today's costs; the importer expects its SQLite file to have the column like the other later columns (a file from before this version must be opened by this version once first).)*

[x] **[Low] The same names are different on SQLite but the same on MariaDB.** **NEW (13th scan)** `MariaDbSchema` builds every table with `COLLATE utf8mb4_unicode_ci`, which ignores case **and accents**, and `categories.name` is `UNIQUE` (line 63); on SQLite the same column is a plain `UNIQUE` text (`SqliteSchema` line 56). On MariaDB the categories "Trà" and "Tra" (or "Cà phê" and "Ca phe") are one name, on SQLite they are two, and the product-name check in the app (`existsByNameInCategory`) answers differently on the two databases. Copying a SQLite shop into MariaDB (`SqliteToMariaDbImporter`) can therefore stop on a duplicate key for data that was valid before. **(confirmed from the two schema files; a failing import was not tried)** Decide one rule (accent-insensitive on both is the friendlier one, see 3.18 and 1.9), make the SQLite side follow it with the `VN_KEY` function, and let the importer report the two clashing names instead of failing half-way. **DONE in code, session 123.** *Rule decided: accent-insensitive on both, exactly MariaDB's own rule (case, accents and trailing spaces do not count; spaces inside a name do; `đ` is its own letter).* **Checked on a real MariaDB 10.11 first:** `utf8mb4_unicode_ci` has `Trà` = `Tra` and `Cà phê` = `Ca phe`, but `Đỗ` is **not** `Do` (so the `VN_KEY` / `TextNormalizer.searchKey` fold the item suggested, which turns `đ` into `d`, would have been stricter than the server and is kept for search boxes only), and it ignores trailing spaces but not double spaces inside a name. `TextNormalizer.collationKey` is that rule in Java; it was compared with the server for 703 spellings of every Vietnamese letter in both cases, composed and with separate marks (12 614 equal pairs on both sides, 0 differences) and for 2 704 ordered pairs of names inserted into a `categories` table made like `MariaDbSchema`'s (0 differences). **What changed:** (1) `CategoryDAO.rejectDuplicateName` uses `TextNormalizer.uniqueKey` (`collationKey` of `key`, a little stricter than the server because extra spaces never make another name, 3.18), so `Trà` / `Tra` is refused with the same plain message on both databases; before, SQLite took both and MariaDB answered with the server's own duplicate-key text. The SQLite side is the code check on purpose and **not** a `VN_KEY` unique index (3.18, 14th pass: an index on a function breaks every other tool that opens the file, and the function must exist on every connection). (2) `SqliteToMariaDbImporter.checkSource` now looks at `categories` before one row is copied (`clashingCategoryNames`, compared with `collationKey`, so a file MariaDB would accept is never refused) and says which two names clash (`db.import.dupcategory`, both languages); a failing copy half-way is no longer possible for this cause. **Correction to the item:** the product-name check does *not* differ between the databases: `ProductDAO.existsByNameInCategory` compares in Java on both and `products.name` has no unique key, so products stay as they are (accent-sensitive, `ProductNameCheckTest` line 66 says so on purpose); only `categories.name` had the difference. **Tests:** `TextNormalizerTest` +4, `SqliteImportSourceCheckTest` +3, new `MariaDbCategoryNameRuleTest` (1, MariaDB-only: every pair of 52 spellings, server verdict against `collationKey`). **Not done:** a SQLite shop that already holds `Trà` and `Tra` keeps both until the owner renames one (the app cannot merge two categories); only the import refuses it, with the two names.

[x] **[Low] The SQLite-to-MariaDB import opens the old file read-write and never looks at its version or its health.** **NEW (22nd scan)** `SqliteToMariaDbImporter.importIfRequested` opens `jdbc:sqlite:<file>` with the default (read-write) mode although its comment promises the file "is only read, never changed": opening a file with a leftover `-wal`/`-journal` lets SQLite replay or delete it, and a file that is not a Lunavera one but is a database is still touched. It checks only that a `users` table exists. It does not read `PRAGMA user_version`: a file from a **newer** app version carries columns and meanings this build ignores (they are dropped without a word), a file from before the money migration (version 0) holds `REAL` prices that MariaDB rounds into whole dong, and it does not run `PRAGMA integrity_check` / `foreign_key_check` although the foreign keys are switched off for the copy, so orphan rows (an order line whose product is gone) are copied and only fail later on the first screen that joins them. **(confirmed in code; not run, no MariaDB here)** Open the file read-only (`SQLiteConfig.setReadOnly(true)`), refuse a version above `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` with a message, warn on version 0, run `integrity_check` and `foreign_key_check` before the copy and stop with the first problem rows; test with a hand-made file for each case. **DONE in code (Session 109):** `SqliteToMariaDbImporter.openSourceReadOnly` (`SQLiteConfig.setReadOnly(true)`) and `checkSource`, run after the "has a `users` table" test and before the first row is copied. A version above `SchemaMigrations.LATEST_VERSION` (2; not `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION`, which is 1 and is only the money migration, so a valid version-2 file would have been refused) stops the import with `db.import.newer`; a failed `integrity_check`, or a file that cannot be read to the end, stops it with `db.import.damaged` (SQLite's own text goes to the log only, so the screen filter does not hide the message); rows found by `foreign_key_check` stop it with `db.import.orphans` (the count and the first 5 as `table #row -> parent`); version 0 is copied with a warning in the log only, because the SQLite version of the app upgrades such a file losslessly. A refusal throws like any failed import, so the app stops with the message instead of carrying on with an empty shop. Tests: `SqliteImportSourceCheckTest` (8, files made by hand, including a damaged page and a "file is byte for byte unchanged" check). `docs/DATABASE.md` says so. Compiled against a stand-in for `SQLiteConfig`; **not run**. Not tested: a leftover `-wal` or `-journal` file next to the old file.

A passphrase backup is also decrypted and checked. The success message now says "Đã kiểm tra". The automatic backup goes through the same code. New tests: a truncated zip fails, a fresh backup is kept. NOT run: they need the database driver.)*

[x] **[Med] After the computer's clock has been wrong, no automatic backup is made until the clock catches up with the wrong date.** **NEW (session 84)** `BackupService.isDue` is "no automatic backup yet, or the newest one is at least 24 hours old", with the age taken as `Duration.between(newestFileTime, now)`. A backup made while the clock was ahead (a till whose date was set to next year, a wrong day after a flat battery, a clock set forward to test something) has a modified time in the future, so the age is negative and the answer stays "not due" until the real date reaches it, which can be months or years; meanwhile `pruneAutomatic` keeps the newest-by-modified-time, which is that future file, and deletes the real recent ones first as the count goes over 14. **(confirmed in code; not run, but the arithmetic is the whole check)** Treat a newest backup that is more than a few minutes in the future as "not a usable date": due now. Order the backups for pruning by the stamp in the file name (`auto-yyyyMMdd-HHmmss`), which the program wrote itself, or by the oldest of name and modified time, so one wrong date cannot make a file immortal. Tests with a file whose time is 400 days ahead: `isDue` is true, and `pruneAutomatic` removes the wrong-dated one only after the older real ones are over the limit, or first (decide, and test that). *(DONE in code, session 89: `isDue` ignores an automatic backup whose file time is more than 10 minutes ahead of the clock, so a wrong-clock copy no longer blocks the next backup. Pruning ranks a backup whose name stamp is in the future as the oldest, so it is deleted first once there are over 14 and never outlives the real recent ones (decision: wrong-dated first). Tests: 400 days ahead gives `isDue` true; two prune tests with an injected `now`. NOT run: they need the database driver.)*

[x] **[Low] The birthday suggestion came back on every order of that day.** *(DONE in code, session 150; the database tests are written but NOT run, no SQLite driver here; the SQL was run on real SQLite and the rules tests were run.)* The item said: `BirthdayRules.isBirthday` only compared today with the stored date, nothing recorded that the 10% was already given, so a customer who ordered three times on their birthday was offered it three times; and anybody who knew a number could get the badge. It asked for a rule (once a day per customer is the usual one), a remembered day (for example `customers.birthday_used_on`, set inside the payment transaction), the badge hidden afterwards, and optionally a dedicated Birthday button. **Rule D25 (mine; the owner has not answered; please confirm): once a day per customer, by its own button.** The payment box shows a "Birthday 10%" button only when the attached customer's birthday is today and it has not been given today. Paying with it writes today's date into the new column `customers.birthday_used_on` (added through `LATER_COLUMNS`, the MariaDB table and the importer; no schema version change) inside the payment's own transaction, in both payment paths (a new order and the payment of an open order, so also a split bill): `CustomerDAO.useBirthday` reads the customer, refuses when it is not their birthday, and writes the day only `WHERE birthday_used_on IS NULL OR birthday_used_on <> today`, so a stale screen or a second till cannot give it twice (0 rows updated gives "already given today" and the WHOLE payment is rolled back, no order is kept). The badge then says "birthday today: the discount was already given" and the button is gone. The service also refuses a birthday payment with no customer attached or a discount that is not exactly the button's amount (`BirthdayRules.problemToUse`), so a typed amount is an ordinary discount again (the button's flag is cleared when the amount is edited). Every use is one audit line (`BIRTHDAY_DISCOUNT_USED`: customer, order, who; the date of birth is not written in it). Merging two customers keeps the LATER given-day (a merge cannot give it again), anonymising clears it. **Code:** `BirthdayRules` (`isOffered`, `usedToday`, `discountFor`, `problemToUse`, new badge texts), `Customer.birthdayUsedOn`, `CheckoutService.Payment` (new flag `birthday`, `withBirthday()`, the old 10-argument constructor still works), `Order.birthdayDiscount` (not saved), `OrderDAO.payExistingOrder(.., birthday)` and the insert path, `PromotionPanel` (the button; takes the customer), two one-line changes in `OrderController` (still exactly 1536 lines), 9 new texts in both languages. **Run here:** `BirthdayUseRulesTest` (7, no database) passes; the new SQL on real SQLite (given once, refused on the repeat the same day, allowed the next birthday, merge keeps the later day, anonymise clears it, an anonymous customer cannot be read for use); 448 no-database tests pass and the only failures are the ones of the unchanged upload (the two controllers already over their size ceiling, 4 `ProductCsvTest` in one JVM, the old `expense.cat.`). **Written, not run:** `BirthdayDiscountTest` (10: given and recorded, never twice the same day with the second order not saved, a stale screen cannot give it again, a plain discount is not tracked, refused on another day with no order kept, no customer or a wrong amount, given again the next birthday, an open order, the audit line, merge and anonymise); the button was not seen on a screen. **What this does NOT stop:** the plain percentage buttons still work for any promotion and are not tracked, so a cashier can still type 10% by hand; and a cashier can still attach someone else's customer whose birthday is today to give a stranger the discount. The use is now limited to once a day per customer and every use is in the audit trail with the cashier, so an owner can see a pattern, but this is not a lock. A refund of an order that used it does not give the birthday discount back.

[x] **[Med] The app trusts the PC's time zone.** **Ticked 2026-11-07 (clean-up scan): the code, tests and messages this item names were found in the source by reading; nothing was run (no Maven, no JavaFX, no database), so the checks in `TODO.md` section 0 still apply.** **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** `AppClock.now()` is `LocalDateTime.now()`, and every date in the database is stored as zone-less text (`DbTime.write`). Vietnam has no summer time, but a PC that was set up with the wrong zone (a fresh Windows install left on UTC or Pacific, a laptop that came back from a trip, "set the zone automatically" picking another country) shifts every sale by hours: the day totals, the shift close, the reservations and the idle times move together, and on a shop with two tills on MariaDB (4.14) one till on the wrong zone mixes two clocks in the same table. The start-up `ClockCheck` of 3.10 helps only partly: it warns when the clock is more than 10 minutes behind the newest saved order, so a zone that was wrong from the first day (no earlier order to compare with), or one that jumps ahead, passes unnoticed. **(confirmed in code: `AppClock` has no zone; not seen in use)** All "now" calls already go through `AppClock` (`AppClockUsageTest` keeps it so), so one change is enough: a shop zone (default `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh`, kept with the other settings, see 4.49) used by `AppClock.now()` and `today()`, plus a warning at start when the PC's zone offset is different from it ("Giờ của máy tính khác giờ Việt Nam"). New `util/ShopZone`: the zone is `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh` unless `shop.zone=<zone id>` is set in `ui-settings.properties` in the data folder (per computer, NOT in the database, because `AppClock` is read before the database is open; an unknown id is ignored with a log line); `ShopZone.install()` is the first thing `LunaveraApplication.init()` does. Until a zone is set (all tests) `AppClock` is the PC's zone exactly as before, so the existing tests behave the same on any machine. The start-up also shows `ShopZone.mismatchWarning` when the PC's offset differs from the shop's at that moment, with both times ("Máy tính đang dùng múi giờ UTC (UTC+00:00), bây giờ là 18:00 02/11/2026 ... giờ cửa hàng ... 01:00 03/11/2026"), because this fix **assumes the PC's clock is right as a moment in time** (what automatic time keeps even with a wrong zone); a PC whose clock was set by hand to local time under the wrong zone would now be shifted, and the warning lets the owner see that at once. [x] **[Med] Non-breaking spaces, zero-width characters and direction marks slip past every cleaner.** **DONE 2026-11-03:** `TextNormalizer.clean` (every space separator to a space, format characters dropped, emoji joiners kept) and `strip` (joiners dropped too, for numbers and codes), used by `nfc` (so `key`, `uniqueKey`, `searchKey` and every saved name), `ValidationUtil` (phone, email; `+84 (0) 901 111 222` and `+840901111222` now read), `MoneyParser.parseDong`, `VoucherRules.normalise`, `LoyaltyPolicy.parsePoints`, and the text cleaners (`StaffRules`, `IngredientRules`, `ProductCode`, `OrderNote`, `StockRules`, `PaymentDetails`); one shared list of awkward characters in `InvisibleCharactersTest`. Not done: `MariaDbSchema` search on MariaDB compares the stored column, which is clean for names saved from now on, not for names saved before. Original text: **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** Text copied from Zalo, a web page or the iPhone contacts often carries U+00A0 (non-breaking space), U+202F, U+200B (zero-width space) or U+200E and U+202A to U+202C (direction marks). Checked by running the code: a phone number with one of them is refused as invalid with no hint why (`ValidationUtil.isValidPhone`, because Java's `trim()` and `\s` do not match them), so is an email with a trailing one; `+84 (0) 901 111 222` and `+840901111222` are refused too; `MoneyParser` accepts U+00A0 but not U+202F or U+200B; and `TextNormalizer.key`, `uniqueKey` and `searchKey` keep them, so a product or category named with a non-breaking space is not seen as a duplicate of the same name with a normal space, and a search for "tra dao" does not find it (the same gap on the SQL side, `DatabaseConfig.searchKeySql`). Fix in one place: a `TextNormalizer.clean` that turns every Unicode space separator into a normal space and drops format characters (zero-width, direction marks, byte-order mark), used before phone, email, money, name keys and search, and on save of names; accept an optional `(0)` after `+84`. A voucher code (customers receive them in a Zalo message, so they are pasted) and a points amount with one of these characters are refused as well (`VoucherRules.normalise` gives no code, `LoyaltyPolicy.parsePoints` gives no number). Put the awkward inputs in one shared test list so every parser is tried with them. *(Found independently by 2 scans; the texts are joined here.)* A second scan confirmed it for money by running it: `50<U+00A0>000` gives 50000 but `50<U+202F>000` (the narrow no-break space that newer spreadsheet and phone locales put there) is refused with the "digits only" message. Read every Unicode space separator (`\p{Zs}`) as a space in `MoneyParser.parseDong` and the other money boxes that use it, and add both characters to `MoneyParserTest`.

[x] **[Med] A reprinted receipt looks exactly like the original, and nothing records the reprint.** **DONE in code 2026-11-03 (not run under Maven; the receipt window and a real printer were not seen):** `orders.print_count`, `RECEIPT_REPRINTED`, `ReceiptPrintDAO`, the mark on the screen/A4 and thermal receipt; see `CHANGELOG.md`. **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** In the order history "In hóa đơn" prints the same page as at the till: the same title, the same time, no "copy" mark and no count (`HoaDonController` shows `getPaidAt()` and nothing says it is a second print), and `AuditAction` has no reprint action. A second identical receipt can be used to claim a refund twice, to charge a customer's expense twice, or to hide that a till was opened again. The usual rule in a shop: a reprint carries "BẢN IN LẠI / COPY" and the number of the print, and the reprint is written to the audit log with who did it. Add `orders.print_count` (migration on both databases), the mark on the receipt (both languages, thermal and A4 layout, `ThermalReceipt`), a `RECEIPT_REPRINTED` audit action, and a test that the first print has no mark and the second has.

[x] **[Med] A photo taken with a phone can show sideways, and it carries the place it was taken.** **DONE in code 2026-11-03 (run only with the library-free tests and the JDK's ImageIO, not under Maven or in a JavaFX window):** `PictureCleaner` + `JpegMetadata`, see `CHANGELOG.md`; pictures stored before this keep their metadata. **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** `ImageStorage.storeNewFile` copies the chosen file byte for byte (`Files.copy`), checks the size and that JavaFX can open it, and goes by the file name's ending. Two things follow. (1) A phone JPEG is usually stored landscape with an EXIF "orientation" tag; JavaFX's `Image` ignores that tag, so a portrait photo of a drink appears turned on its side on the menu card and in the product list (searched for `exif`, `orientation` and `rotate` in the code and the notes: nothing). (2) The same EXIF block normally holds the GPS position, the phone model and the time; those files sit in `data/images` and travel inside every backup and in any copy of the folder given to a helper or an accountant. **(confirmed from the code; the orientation effect is as far as I know and was not run, JavaFX is needed)** Fix: when a picture is added, read it with `ImageIO`, apply the orientation, scale it down to the largest size the screens use (for example 1,024 px on the long side), and write a new JPEG (or PNG when it has transparency) that has no metadata; keep the 8 MB and pixel limits on the input. The read of the tag is about 40 lines (the APP1 segment of a JPEG). Tests with a small hand-made JPEG that carries orientation 6 and a GPS tag: the stored file has swapped width and height, no APP1 segment, and a source with no EXIF still works; pictures already stored are left alone (offer the same clean-up for them with one button, like the unused-pictures one). *(Found independently by 2 scans; the texts are joined here.)* A second scan adds the other side of the same code: a picture above 8 MB, above 16 million pixels or longer than 8,000 pixels is refused (`MAX_PICTURE_BYTES`, `MAX_PICTURE_PIXELS`, `MAX_PICTURE_SIDE`), so a 48 or 50 megapixel phone photo fails with a message and the owner has to resize it by hand, while a smaller one is stored at full size in the images folder and in every backup. So re-encode on save: longest side at most 1,024 pixels, JPEG (PNG when it has transparency), rotation applied, other metadata dropped, and keep the checks only as a safety limit for the file read. Needs `requires java.desktop;` in `module-info.java` (for `javax.imageio`) and a larger jlink image (item 6.53), so check both.

[x] **[Low] Two tills can give the same take-away number.** **DONE 2026-11-03:** new table `queue_counters (day, last_no)`; `OrderDAO.nextQueueNumber` raises the day's row with one `UPDATE` (the row lock serialises tills until the order commits); the first order of a day creates the row after the highest number already given (so an upgrade in the middle of a day goes on correctly); a lost race on the key is retried. Not run on MariaDB. Original text: **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** `OrderDAO.nextQueueNumber` reads `MAX(queue_no) + 1` for the day inside the inserting transaction, and the comment accepts that two tills opening a take-away order at the same moment "could in theory" get the same number. On MariaDB with two tills each transaction sees only its own snapshot until it commits, so the clash needs only two orders within a second or two, and two customers then wait for number 14. There is no unique index. **(confirmed in code; the accepted risk is written in the method's comment)** Cheap fix: take a row lock first (`SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on a one-row counter kept per day, or an `INSERT ... ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE n = LAST_INSERT_ID(n + 1)` counter table) so the number comes from the counter, not from the orders. If the owner is content with the shout-at-the-counter rule, tick this as "decided, not fixed" and say so here. Merge note: when the branches were merged, the per-day counter table `queue_counters` of this item was NOT kept; the take-away number race is closed by the `queue_day` key and the retry of TODO 2.98, which also follows the shop's day start (4.50).

A shop that copied a wrong value into the shortcut ended up with a till that never locks and nobody told.
   Done (session 190): `IdleTracker` takes only a plain number (digits with an optional fraction, a comma counts as a point) from 1 to 1440 minutes (`MAX_MINUTES`), or exactly `0` on purpose; anything else (`NaN`, `-5`, `Infinity`, `1e9`, `abc`, `0,5`, `5d`, `0x10`, `1441`, a very long text) gives the 15-minute default and one WARNING that names the refused value (cut at 40 characters). The deliberate `0` is a WARNING too ("switched off on purpose"), also when it comes from Settings, so both show in the support file. The same rules apply to the owner's setting passed to `IdleTracker.limit`: Settings already refuses more than 480 or less than 0, so a negative or above-a-day value there (a hand-edited database) now gives the default and a warning instead of "off". The limit is read every ten seconds, so each distinct warning is written once (`forgetWarnings()` is for tests). `IdleLogout` logs its own start-up "switched off" at WARNING instead of INFO. README line on auto logout updated. **Changed on purpose:** `0.5` and other values below 1 minute are no longer taken (they were a way to test, and the item lists `0,5` as a mistake); `1,5` still gives 90 seconds. Tests: `IdleTrackerTest` rewritten around this (good values with no warning, 15 refused values each with one warning that names them, `0`, `0.0` and `00`, the warning written once, a very long value, the owner's setting), and the existing `ShopRulesMoreTest` cases still hold.

## 4. Features to add (most important first)

> **8th pass, status of the open features.** Done here: 4.1 (found already complete) and the `before-restore` part of 4.28. Everything else still open needs at least one of: a **new screen or dialog** (JavaFX cannot be loaded in the environment these passes run in, so a screen could not be seen or clicked), **new tables or columns in both database definitions** plus the importer, a numbered migration and tests on both databases (cannot be run there), or a **decision from the owner / an accountant**. Grouped: screens + new tables: 4.2 audit log, 4.5 stock receiving log, 4.6 shift close, 4.9 order notes, 4.10 table transfer / merge / split bill, 4.18 table management, 4.25 take-away orders, 4.31 transfer reference; screens only: 4.7 settings, 4.8 merge customers, 4.12 employee screen, 4.15 first-run wizard (the admin-account part exists, see 2A.5), 4.16 spending points, 4.19 reorder level (needs a column), 4.24 database health line, 4.26 set the transfer QR in the app, 4.27 clean up unused pictures, 4.29 former staff; owner or accountant first: 4.11 promotions, 4.22 Vietnamese invoicing rules; hardware: 4.13 kitchen ticket printer; 4.14 several tills: the MariaDB mode (1.5) is the base for it, but two tills at once have not been tried. What would let more of these be built and checked: allow `repo.maven.apache.org` in the sandbox network settings so `mvn clean test` runs, and give a way to start the app with a display (or accept that screens are checked by you).

On MariaDB the backup is the `mysqldump` file and the restore button only shows the phpMyAdmin steps (open item 1.8).

Lines are written for: a discount given with a payment (amount and the reason typed), a paid order refunded (with or without the goods going back to stock), a table emptied with an open order, an open take-away order cancelled, a changed price, cost or typed-over stock number of a product, an account created, its role changed, locked, unlocked, its password reset by an administrator or changed by its owner (never the password or hash), a staff record deleted or made anonymous, a customer merged, made anonymous or deleted, a shift closed, a backup made by hand and a backup restored at start-up. New columns `orders.cancelled_by`, `cancelled_at`, `cancel_reason`: set by a refund, by emptying a table and by cancelling a take-away order. A refund now asks for a reason (at least 3 characters) in one new dialog, `RefundDialog`, that replaces the old three-button question. Administrator screen `AuditLogWindow` (sidebar button "Nhật ký thao tác", administrators only): filter by day, kind of action, person and a word (number, name, reason), newest first, paged. The personal parts are removed from older lines when a person is made anonymous (`scrubActor`, `scrubTarget`, in the same transaction as 4.29 and 4.8), and a staff record with an audit line or a cancelled order counts as having history, so it cannot be deleted. Limits: nothing is written for a new product, a deleted product, a changed salary, a changed shop setting, an automatic backup, or a restore that is only scheduled (the line would be lost with the replaced database; the monthly text file still records the schedule); the actor of a line written while nobody is signed in is "(system)"; the text filter is case-insensitive for plain letters only on SQLite; there is no export of the log and no pruning; the reports still count a refund on the order's original day (3.13 is the rule decision) although `cancelled_at` is now stored.)*
       tables emptied with an open order, product price/stock edits, accounts locked or changed, backups and restores. Paying the rest later is an ordinary payment. All three write the audit log (`TABLE_TRANSFERRED`, `TABLES_MERGED`, `BILL_SPLIT`) and are refused when nobody is signed in or the order changed on another till. Not done: moving one line to another table, splitting a discounted order's discount (a split's discount is typed on the part being paid), merging a take-away order. Tests: `TableMoveRulesTest` 6, `TableMoveTest` 11. `NoteNameCell` and `TableMovePanel` were taken out of `OrderController`, whose size ceiling is now 1664.)*

16. [x] **[Med] Spending loyalty points.** **NEW (3rd scan)** Points are earned and shown with a tier, but nothing in the payment flow can use them, so they have no value for the customer. Add a redemption (for example 1 point = 1.000đ off), recorded **OWNER (2026-10-28, D5): 1 point = 1.000đ, at least 20.000đ (20 points) at once, at most half of the order, a refund gives the points back, a cashier may apply them, and they are not counted as a discount for the 10% limit. Session 143 changed the rule to match: the minimum is now `LoyaltyPolicy.MIN_POINTS = 20` (it was none); the rest was already so.**
        on the order and the customer's balance in the same transaction as the payment, with the cancel-order rule in edge case 3.7 settled first. **(confirmed: no redemption code found)** **DONE in code, session 135 (rule D5 was not written down anywhere in the project files, so this is the rule I applied: change a number in `LoyaltyPolicy` to change it).** *The rule:* **1 point = 1.000đ off** (`VND_PER_POINT_SPENT`; points are still earned at 1 per 10.000đ paid, so the customer gets 10% back); points may pay **at most 50% of the bill after the cashier's discount** (`MAX_PERCENT_OF_BILL`, whole points, so a sale is never paid entirely with points and no total can reach 0); only a **customer attached to the sale** can spend (typing a new name and phone at the till makes a customer with 0 points); **earned points are worked out on what was actually paid** (after the points); a **refund gives the spent points back** (see 3.7 below). *Payment dialog:* a "Dùng điểm" row under the discount: the customer's points (read again from the database when the dialog opens), the rate, the most that can be used, a "Tối đa" button; the total, change and quick-cash buttons follow what is typed; a number that is not allowed (more than they have, more than half the bill, not a whole number) stops the payment with the reason in plain words; with no customer attached or no points the row says so. *Saved:* `orders.points_redeemed` (how many) and `orders.points_value` (what they were worth then, so a later change of the rate does not rewrite old orders); the value is **also inside `orders.discount`** and the reason says "Dùng n điểm" (after the cashier's own reason, if any), so `total = subtotal - discount` still holds and the reports, the profit allocation, the discounts total and the receipt's discount line needed no change; the printed receipt has a "Điểm đã dùng: n" line. *Transaction:* `OrderDAO.insert` and `payExistingOrder` take the points with `UPDATE customers SET loyalty_points = loyalty_points - ? WHERE id = ? AND loyalty_points >= ? AND anonymised_at IS NULL` in the same transaction as the sale: when another till spent them first, nothing is saved (stock, order and balance all as before) and the message says so. `CheckoutService` checks the same rule first with the plain-words messages and applies the staff discount limit (2.3) to the cashier's own discount **only**: the customer's points are not the cashier's to give. *Refund (the rule 3.7 needed settled):* `cancelPaidOrder` takes back the points the order earned (floor 0, as decided in session 43) **and then** returns the points it spent (capped at the largest number the column holds); a customer made anonymous since gets nothing back (their record keeps no points, 4.8). *Both databases:* two columns through the existing later-columns list (SQLite and MariaDB alike), the MariaDB `CREATE TABLE`, and the importer; the guarded spend and give-back statements were run on a real MariaDB 10.11 (1 row when allowed, 0 rows when refused, capped, 0 rows for an anonymous record). *Tests (20 new):* `LoyaltyRedemptionRulesTest` (6, no database), `PointsRedemptionTest` (14: spent and recorded, no points leaves the columns empty, staff limit does not apply, a cashier's discount plus points, more than the balance, more than half the bill, no customer and negative, another till spent them first, an open order, refund gives them back, refund floor, refund without points, refund for an anonymous customer, profit and discounts in the report); `LaterColumnsExistTest` lists the two columns. *Not done / limits:* no setting screen for the rate or the 50% (a code change); no "points" column in the order history list or the CSV (the reason text says it); the dashboard; a loyalty cost line in the report (the discount total includes it); the sales screen shows the customer's points only in the dialog; the payment dialog and the receipt were parsed and read, not opened (JavaFX is not available here), so the new row is for you to look at. *(Name check 2026-11-08: `LoyaltyPolicy.MIN_POINTS` no longer exists; the minimum is now the shop setting `ShopRules.minPoints()`, read through `LoyaltyPolicy.minPoints()`.)*

AND stock >= ?` and the line is written in **one transaction** (nothing changes when either fails); the quantity is stored negative (`stockBefore` → `stockAfter` reads like the other lines), the **reason is required** (cleaned like the other free text, at most 100 characters, kept in the existing `note` column), **who and when** come from the session and `AppClock` like every log line, and `unit_cost` holds the **product's cost price at that moment** so the loss does not change when the price is edited later (a product with no cost price gives a line with no cost, which the report counts apart and never prices at 0, as in 4.20). More than the stock is refused with the number in stock (also when a sale at another till takes the last pieces in between); all of the stock may be written off; administrators only, like the rest of the log. **No schema change, no migration** (`stock_log.kind` is free text; the importer and both schemas are untouched). **Screen:** the "Nhập kho" dialog (`StockLogDialog`) has a switch "Nhập hàng / Hao hụt" over the same form: in waste mode the cost, supplier and note rows give way to a reason box (four suggested reasons, own words allowed), the button reads "Ghi hao hụt", the status line says what the loss cost, and the log table shows the new type "Hao hụt" next to receipts and edits. **Report:** `ReportDAO.waste(from, to)` (lines, pieces, cost, lines without a cost price; by the day of the log line) and `WasteText`: a line under the summary of the report window ("Hao hụt: n dòng, n sản phẩm, chi phí X", with the note about lines without a cost price; left out when there was none) and three rows in the CSV (always written). The **gross profit above it is before waste** (waste is a loss on top of what was sold; the figure is not reduced). **Checked on a real MariaDB 10.11:** the SQL of `ReportDAO.waste` on a `stock_log` made like `MariaDbSchema`'s gave 3 lines, 15 pieces, 29 000 đ, 1 without a cost for a day with the edges 23:59:59 in and 00:00:00 out, other kinds and other days left out; on SQLite the same through `WasteLogTest`. **Tests (15 new):** `WasteLogTest` (11: the line, the cost of that moment, no cost, too many, reason and quantity rules, long reason, product gone, administrators only, the report's lines/pieces/cost, day edges and other kinds, an empty range, profit unchanged), `WasteTextTest` (4). **Not done:** waste per ingredient (4.41); waste by an employee who is not an administrator (the dialog is on the product screen, which is the administrator's); a fixed list of reasons with a total per reason (the reason is free text in `note`, so the report shows one total, not one per reason; a `reason` column would need schema version 4); the dashboard; the refund-restock line (2.66) is not touched. The dialog and the report window were parsed and read, not opened (JavaFX is not available here), so the switch and the new report line are for you to look at.

[x] **[Med] Lock the till and switch cashier with a short PIN.** **NEW (11th scan)** The only ways to change who is selling are a full logout and a login with the password (600,000-round hash, throttled), and the idle logout after 15 minutes closes the screen and every dialog, which the cart autosave survives but the cashier's patience may not. In a busy cafe two or three people share one till; they tend to stay signed in as one account, and then `orders.paid_by` (2.47) and every discount reason name the wrong person. **(reasoned)** A "Khóa" button (and the idle timeout) that locks the screen while keeping the cart, and a 4-6 digit PIN per account (stored hashed, with its own throttle) to unlock or to switch; the PIN must never be enough to sign in from the login screen, and an admin screen stays behind the password. **DONE in code (Session 136), with rule D7 as chosen here (change it if the owner wants otherwise):** (1) only *staff* accounts have a PIN; an administrator never does, an administrator's till opens only with the administrator's password, and a PIN can never take over an administrator's session; (2) **a PIN never signs anybody in**: `PinService` is not used by the login screen (a test reads `LoginController` and `AuthenticationService` and fails if they mention it; a PIN typed at the login is a wrong password) and it refuses to work unless somebody is already signed in with a password as staff; (3) 4 to 6 digits, not all the same and not a run up or down (`PinRules`), set by the person themselves in the sidebar ("Mã PIN", `PinSetupDialog`) after giving the password, stored hashed like a password in `users.pin_hash`; (4) its own throttle (`LoginThrottle.persistent("pin")`, one wait per account, kept in the database) and a hard limit: after 5 wrong PINs in a row (`users.pin_failures`) the PIN is switched off and must be set again after a password sign-in, so the 10 000 four-digit PINs cannot be tried at the till; (5) an administrator resetting a password, or a former member of staff being made anonymous, also removes the PIN; (6) "Khóa" in every sidebar locks the till: `TillLock` hides and disables the content of every window (the screen is not closed, so the cart and the open order stay as they are) and `LockScreen` waits for the PIN (a list of the cashiers with a PIN, the locker first: the same person unlocks, another cashier takes over and `paid_by` and the discount reasons then name that cashier) or, for an administrator or a staff member without a PIN, for the password; "Đăng xuất" is the other way out, Esc and the window's X do nothing; (7) the idle timeout locks instead of signing out; a till still locked after another idle period is signed out as before. Needs the audit log (4.2) first. **DONE in code, Session 137, not run and not seen on a screen (the Java tests were NOT run; the refund arithmetic and every new SQL statement were):** **What a refund of lines does:** the administrator opens the order in the history, presses "Hoàn từng dòng" and chooses how many pieces of each line to give back, how the money goes back (cash, card, transfer...), whether the goods return to stock (untick for a drink already made) and an optional reason; the total to give back is shown as the numbers change. `RefundDAO.refund` does it in ONE transaction: one row per refunded line in the new table `refunds` (order, line, pieces, `line_amount`, `amount`, method, restocked, reason, who, when), the stock of those pieces only, and the loyalty points in proportion. The order stays PAID. **Rules I chose (the TODO leaves them open):** what is given back for a line is its share of what the customer PAID (line total x order total / subtotal, so the share of the discount, and of the points spent, goes with it), worked out on the pieces refunded so far so that two steps equal one step; all refunds of an order never exceed its total and the last pieces bring it to exactly the total (`RefundMath`); the points the order earned are taken back and the points spent are given back in proportion to the money refunded, ending at exactly what a whole refund moves; an order with refunded lines can no longer be cancelled as a whole (`OrderDAO.cancelPaidOrder` refuses, "refund the remaining lines instead") and a cancelled order cannot have lines refunded. **In the reports (3.13 + 4.37):** `SalesLedger` has a third kind of row per refunded line: negative money on the day the refund was made, NO change to the number of orders or the discounts, the cash part only when it was given back in cash; a new `SalesLedger.lines()` gives the product, category and profit queries only the refunded pieces. The sale stays on its own day. Summary, daily table, products, categories, profit, payment split (the refund comes off the method it was given back by), sales per employee, the shift totals (a cash refund comes out of the drawer's expected cash) and the dashboard revenue and 7-day chart all follow. The owner sets them in a new third tab "Kích cỡ và topping" of the Ingredients window (no new dashboard button: `AdminDashboardController` and `ProductController` are at their size ceilings), with "copy to the category" so a menu is set up once (all or nothing). **On the order line** (four new nullable columns of `order_items`, added by `LATER_COLUMNS` and by the MariaDB table, **no schema_version change**): `size_name`, `size_percent`, `extras` (text `Extra shot=10000;Cream=5000`) and `option_cost`. The names and prices are COPIED at the tap, so a later change of an option moves no old order; `unit_price` already includes the size and extras, so totals, discounts, refunds, revenue and the stock check needed no change. **Till:** tapping a product that has switched-on options opens a box (`OptionPickerDialog`: the usual size is already chosen, extras are ticked, the price of one piece shows live); a product with none adds at once as before. A line is the same line only with the same product, note, size and extras (`Cart`, `OrderItemDiff`, `TableMoveRules`; `SplitBillRules` keeps them on split-off pieces). The cart shows `↳ L, +Extra shot · note`. **Recipe and cost:** a size takes `amount x size_percent / 100` of every recipe line (`SizeScale`, whole units, rounded, never to nothing), also when a refund or a cancel puts it back; the cost of the line is the product cost x the size share + `option_cost`, written with the sale in Java so SQLite and MariaDB round alike. **Not done on purpose:** an extra takes NO ingredient stock (only its cost counts), and the "cups left" estimate counts a normal serving. **Receipts:** the thermal receipt prints the size and extras under the name, the receipt window and the refund screens show `Latte (L, +Extra shot)`. **Report:** a new tab "Kích cỡ và topping" (`ReportDAO.optionSales`, from `SalesLedger.lines()` so refunds are taken off; a size row is the whole drink sold in it, an extra row only what the extra brought in) and its lines in the CSV. **Tests added (45):** `LineOptionsTest` 9, `SizeScaleTest` 4, `OptionRulesTest` 7, `CartOptionsTest` 9 (no database; run here, all pass) and `ProductOptionsDaoTest` 8, `OrderOptionsDaoTest` 8 (database; written, **not run**). **Run here:** the non-JavaFX code compiles; the 352 existing no-database tests give the same result as on the unchanged upload (the same 4 `ProductCsvTest` failures appear only when all run in one JVM, on the original too); `MariaDbSchemaTest` and `SqliteToMariaDbImporterTest` (21) pass; `I18nTest` shows only the old `expense.cat.` failure; every new or changed SQL statement and the report query were run on real SQLite (sized, extra and refunded lines: L 1 piece after one refund, Extra shot 4 pieces). **Not seen:** the picker, the options tab, the report tab and the cart line on a screen. [x] **Warn when Caps Lock is on at the login and password fields.** **NEW (8th scan)** A cashier who types the password with Caps Lock on gets "wrong password", and every wrong try also feeds the login delay (2A.7). Nothing in `src` reads the Caps Lock state (searched). JavaFX has no public call for the key's state: use AWT's `Toolkit.getLockingKeyState(KeyEvent.VK_CAPS_LOCK)` (needs the `java.desktop` module) or watch the typed letters (a capital letter without Shift); show a small "Caps Lock đang bật" line under the field.
   Done (session 106): a "Caps Lock is on" line (orange, hidden and taking no room until needed) under the username/password fields on the login screen and in the password-change, first-run, and backup-passphrase dialogs. JavaFX 21 cannot read the key's state, so `CapsLockState` works it out from typed letters (a capital without Shift, or a small letter with Shift, means on; the Caps Lock key flips a known state; unknown until a letter is typed and again when the window loses focus; Ctrl/Alt/Meta keys and non-A-Z letters are ignored). `CapsLockWarning` wires it to the fields. Tests: `CapsLockStateTest` (6, run here). Not done: the account create/edit form (an admin typing someone else's password, no failed-attempt cost), and nothing is shown while the password is revealed with the eye button (the plain text field is a different control). The screens themselves were not run (no JavaFX here).

At sign-in, for a cashier who is not clocked in, ask once "clock in for this shift?"; at sign-out and idle logout, if still clocked in, ask "clock out?". Both are a single question with a default button and "not now", so the till is never held up.
   Done (session 179): `util/ClockOffer` asks the question (a box with the useful answer as the default button and "Để sau / Not now" as Esc; the database is read off the screen thread) and `service/ClockOfferRules` decides who is asked. **Sign-in:** after the forced password box, a member of the staff (role Employee) who is not clocked in is asked "Vào ca?"; the administrator is not. **Sign-out:** the four "Đăng xuất" handlers (`AdminScreen`, `OrderController`, `CustomerController`, `EmployeeController`) ask "Ra ca?" first when the person is still clocked in, then the backup question as before, then sign out; "Not now", a database failure or a failed clock-out never stop the sign-out, and a second click while the question is open is ignored. **Lock screen:** "Đăng xuất" there asks the same question; another cashier who opens the till with a PIN is asked to clock in. **Idle sign-out:** the session still ends first (nothing is held open); the "signed out automatically" box then also asks "clock out now?" when the person was clocked in (`TimeClockDAO.openFor` / `clockOutFor`, which work for the person just signed out and are refused for anybody else while a different person is signed in). A stretch that already looks forgotten (the 16-hour limit) is not offered a plain clock-out, because it would be written as worked time; the administrator closes it. 8 texts in both languages, no database change. Tests: `ClockOfferRulesTest` 6, `ClockOfferSourceTest` 5, `ClockAtSignOutTest` 3 (real SQLite), and `ClockOffer` added to the JavaFX list of `NoJavaFxInRulesTest`.
