[x] **[Low] Enable SQLite write-ahead logging.** **NEW (3rd scan)** `getConnection` set `foreign_keys` and `busy_timeout` per connection, but not `journal_mode = WAL` (and `synchronous = NORMAL`; **since TODO 2.92 the setting is `FULL`, because NORMAL under WAL can take back the last paid sales in a power cut**). WAL lets readers (the dashboard, the session check) work while the autosave writes. **(inferred benefit, not measured)** *(DONE in code, 13th pass: `DatabaseConfig.initialize()` ends with `enableWriteAheadLog()`: `PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL` on SQLite, last of all so the automatic pre-migration backup is still taken in the old mode; if the switch is refused (network drive, locked file) it logs a warning and carries on, never fails the start. Only after it worked does `getConnection` also send `PRAGMA synchronous = NORMAL` (safe with WAL, a durability risk without it). `reset()` clears the flag. Backups use `VACUUM INTO` (a plain file, tested by `aBackupOfAWalDatabaseIsStillAPlainValidFile`), the restore code deletes `-journal`, `-wal` and `-shm` before swapping the file, and the legacy-copy code refuses a folder that has a `-wal`. Docs (README, `docs/RELEASE.md`, `docs/DATABASE.md`): never copy `lunavera.db` by hand while the app is open, and delete `-wal`/`-shm` when putting a file back by hand. `DatabaseConfigWalTest` (4 tests). Caveat: WAL does not work on some network or synced folders (OneDrive, Dropbox); then the app logs a warning and stays in the normal mode. If anything fails on the first `mvn test`, look first at the two tests that compare the size of `lunavera.db` (`BackupSafetyTest`, `BackupServiceTest.aBrokenPendingRestoreFailsSafelyAndIsNotRetriedForever`): a checkpoint can change the main file size. See section 0 for the check on Windows.)*

24. [x] **[Med] Add CHECK constraints to the schema.** **NEW (5th scan)** The tables have only `NOT NULL`/`UNIQUE`/foreign keys **(confirmed from the CREATE TABLE text)**. Nothing in the database refuses a negative price or stock, an order item with quantity 0 or less, a discount above the subtotal, or a status such as `'paid'` that no code understands. *(13th pass: **SQLite is done**: `SchemaMigrations.ensureDataRules` creates insert and update triggers that refuse a negative price, cost or stock, an order line with quantity 0 or less or negative money, an order with negative money or a discount above the subtotal, an unknown order status, an unknown user role or status, an unknown table status, and negative loyalty points. Triggers instead of `CHECK` because SQLite cannot add a `CHECK` to an existing table without rebuilding it, and one mechanism then covers new and old databases. The allowed values are written from the enums (`OrderStatus`, `Role`, `AccountStatus`, `TableStatus`), so they cannot drift. Safe at every start (`IF NOT EXISTS`); a rule is skipped, with a warning that gives only the rule and a row count plus the query that lists the rows, while rows already in the database break it (otherwise every later update of such a row would fail), and quietly when its table or column is missing. I read the code first so no real flow trips a rule: stock is only decreased with `stock >= quantity`, loyalty points are clamped at 0, a paid order is written in one statement that sets subtotal, discount and total together. The rules were run in Python's SQLite on the real `CREATE TABLE` text with a normal sale, a cancel, a restock and a loyalty clamp (all pass) and ten bad writes (all refused). `DataRulesTest` (9; NOT run, needs the driver). The MariaDB half was added in part 2 below; one rule is not a rule on either database: `total = subtotal - discount` for a paid order (the code checks it; a trigger would need to be sure no draft order breaks it).)* *(13th pass, part 2: DONE for both databases, so ticked. MariaDB/MySQL now gets the same rules as `CHECK` constraints: `SchemaMigrations.ensureMariaDataRules` runs at every start next to the staff-phone key, skips a rule that already exists (looked up in `information_schema.table_constraints`), skips a rule with a warning (rule name and row count only) while existing rows break it, and tries each rule on its own, so a failure (an old server that cannot add a `CHECK`, a lost connection) is logged and never stops the app from starting. The statement is `ALTER TABLE <t> ADD CONSTRAINT rule_<name> CHECK (NOT (<violation>))`, built by `mariaCheckSql`; `DataRuleSqlTest` (4, run here: pass) fixes its shape and checks that every column the rules name exists in both schemas. **NOT run on a MariaDB server**: the syntax is the documented one for MariaDB 10.2+ and MySQL 8.0.16+, and a rule that fails only produces a warning in the log, so look for \"Data rule ... was not added\" in `data/logs/` on the first start and, in phpMyAdmin, `SHOW CREATE TABLE products` should list `CONSTRAINT rule_products_not_negative CHECK ...`. A server older than MySQL 8.0.16 parses the `CHECK` and ignores it, and gets the statement again at each start. Still not a rule, on either database: `total = subtotal - discount` for a paid order (the code checks it).)*
        Add `CHECK (price >= 0)`, `CHECK (stock >= 0)`, `CHECK (quantity > 0)`, `CHECK (status IN ('OPEN','PAID','CANCELLED'))` and similar. SQLite cannot add a CHECK to an existing table, so this needs the "create new table, copy, drop, rename" migration (see 6.6), done with a backup first and tested on an old database.

[x] **[Med] Clean and share the CSS.** *(DONE in files, session 148; checked by two scripts and one new test, **not seen on a screen** because JavaFX is not available here.)* The item said: 99 identical rules in two or more files, 61 unused classes, merge the shared rules into `common.css`, recheck every screen. **What was true at the start:** the unused-class half was already done in the 13th pass (the real number was 23, not 61, and `CssRulesTest` keeps it at 0; it still passes). The duplicate half was **82 rules in 213 copies** (not 99; the 13th pass had removed dead ones), in four groups of screens: 10 sidebar rules in all six screens, 35 dashboard rules in the administrator and employee sheets, 9 floor-plan table rules in those two and the orders sheet, 28 page-header, card, search and paging rules in the category and account sheets. **What I did:** four shared sheets, each loaded ONLY by the screens that already had its rules, so no screen gets a rule it did not have: `common.css` (the sidebar; six screens), `common-dashboard.css` (administrator, employee and customer screens), `common-tables.css` (the page background and the table-map buttons; the two home screens and the orders screen), `common-management.css` (category, account and staff screens). **81 of the 82 rules moved**; the 130 extra copies are gone. `employee-trangchu.css` became empty (every rule in it was also in the administrator sheet) and was deleted. Rules in `dangnhap.css`, `receipt.css` and `app.css` are untouched. The seven sheets went from 3,720 to 2,758 lines, 501 to 371 rules. Section banners moved with their rules and a banner is dropped from a sheet only when all its rules moved; each file keeps its title banner. **Why not `app.css`, as the old note suggested:** `app.css` is scene level and loses to every screen-level rule whatever its specificity, which can change which rule wins. The shared sheets are instead listed FIRST in each FXML file's `<stylesheets>` (the same element form `donthanhtoan.fxml` already used, with `<?import java.net.URL?>`), at the same level as the screen's own sheet, where a later equal-specificity rule wins, so a screen can still override a shared rule. **The one rule left in two places:** `.search-field:focused` (category and account sheets). The two sheets order it differently against `.search-field:hover`; both set the border colour and a search box can be hovered and focused at once, so merging it would change one screen. It is listed in `KNOWN_DUPLICATES` of the new test with this reason. **How it was checked instead of screenshots:** (1) an order check on the written files: for every screen, every pair of rules whose order changed was tested for "equally specific, same property, different value, could meet on one node" (nodes judged from every FXML node and every `getStyleClass()` call; 11 dashboard classes put on a new `Label` by `styled(...)` were reviewed by hand): 0 problems. (2) a cascade simulation of the original upload against the files on disk, taking each screen's sheet order from its FXML: for every element of the 10 FXML files, in every one of 37 states (none, each of hover/focused/pressed/selected/odd/..., and every pair), the winning value of every property is the same: 32,042 combinations, 0 differences. A control that force-merges `.search-field:focused` is caught (the hovered-and-focused category search box would change its border from `#8B9B80` to `#C9D1C2`). **Test added:** `CssSharedRulesTest` (4, run here: pass; with a planted duplicate and a shared sheet listed after the screen sheet it fails twice): no rule is written in two sheets (apart from the one above), shared sheets come before the screen's own, every shared sheet is loaded by two or more screens and no sheet is loaded by none. `CssRulesTest`, `FxmlControllerCheckTest` and `AdminScreensTest` pass. **Not covered by the simulation:** nodes built in code (the dashboard rows in `DashboardWidgets`, the table-map buttons) and the inside parts of JavaFX controls; these were reasoned from the Java sources, not simulated. **Still worth doing once with eyes:** open the six main screens, the customer and staff screens, and hover and click in the search boxes, and compare with the old build; if a screen loses its styling at start-up, the cause would be the new `<URL>` lines of its FXML file. *(Name check 2026-11-08: `employee-trangchu.css` was deleted in this item (its rules were all in the shared sheets); see the CHANGELOG entry for 6.29.)*

Only `ReportDAO.topProducts` selected a column it does not group by; its name column is now `MAX(COALESCE(p.name, oi.product_name))` (one product per group, so one name: the rows are the same). New `GroupByRulesTest` (3, reads sources only): the number of `GROUP BY` per file must equal the number read, so a new one fails the build until someone reads it (and says what was checked in the test's comment); `topProducts` keeps its `MAX`; the `test-mariadb` job of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` must switch `ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY` on before the tests. [x] **[Low] `QrCodeTest` checks one symbol of version 1; the other 39 versions are untested.** **NEW (16th scan)** `matchesIndependentReferenceEncoder` compares one text (version 1, 21x21) with stored modules; the other tests check sizes, finder patterns and timing. Alignment patterns (version 2 and up), the version information (7 and up) and the interleaving of several Reed-Solomon blocks (version 5 and up) are never looked at, and the receipt QR switches between versions with the length of the text. **To settle it I decoded the output independently:** 86 texts of 1 to 2,331 bytes (every capacity boundary of the table, one real receipt text, one Vietnamese text) were encoded and then read back by a separate Python decoder (format information and its BCH check, both copies; mask; zig-zag read; de-interleaving; Reed-Solomon check of every block; byte-mode payload). **All 86 verified, in all 40 versions, and the two ECC tables equal the standard ones** - so the encoder is right today; nothing protects it from a change. Add a test-side `QrDecoder` (about 120 lines: the same steps) and a test that round-trips a text of each version-changing length, plus one with a text of more than 255 bytes (16-bit count field from version 10). Keep it in `src/test` only.
   Done (session 106): a test-side `QrDecoder` (`src/test`, ~250 lines, own tables for capacity, error-correction blocks and alignment centres) reads the encoder's symbols back: both format copies and their BCH check, level M, mask, both version-information copies (7 and up), zig-zag read, de-interleaving, Reed-Solomon check of every block, byte-mode header with the 8-bit/16-bit count, terminator and padding. `QrCodeAllVersionsTest` round-trips the largest text of every one of the 40 versions and the shortest text of each (capacity boundary +1), an empty text, the 2,331-byte maximum, texts over 255 bytes, Vietnamese text growing through 8+ versions, a transfer note, and all 8 masks at versions 1, 2, 5, 7, 10, 14, 25, 40; the decoder is also shown to refuse a damaged module, format bit and version bit. Ran here (plain JDK): 10 of 10 pass, and four planted encoder bugs (alignment step, version bits, count-field cut-over, block table) each made the tests fail.

[x] **[Low] Give the tables added after item 6.24 the same database-level rules.** **NEW (scans of 2026-11-01)** `SchemaMigrations.DATA_RULES` (triggers on SQLite, `CHECK` constraints on MariaDB) had seven rules and covered only `products`, `order_items`, `orders`, `users`, `dining_tables` and `customers`; nothing protected the tables added since, though the code checks all of them, and for money the rules are the line that still holds when a second till or a hand edit in phpMyAdmin goes around the code.
   Done (session 189): eight rules added to `DATA_RULES` (15 now), each the weakest form the code already keeps, so no legitimate row is refused: `gift_cards_valid` (first amount above 0, balance not below 0), `gift_card_moves_not_zero`, `time_clock_in_before_out` (a clock-out is never before its clock-in), `vouchers_valid` (value above 0, percent at most 100, a known kind, minimum not negative, uses not above the maximum and the maximum above 0), `expenses_amount_positive`, `refunds_not_negative`, `order_payments_positive`, `reservations_valid` (people above 0, a known status from `Reservation.Status`). **Two choices that differ from the item's wording:** `refunds` refuses what is negative, not what is 0, because a refund row legitimately carries 0 pieces (the rest of a line that was paid partly by gift card, `RefundSplit`) or 0 dong (a line a discount made free); and a clock-out equal to the clock-in is not refused (the code never writes one, and a stricter rule could block the import of an old file). The mechanism is unchanged (an existing row that breaks a rule skips that rule with a warning, nothing stops the start), so no schema version and no migration; a database made earlier gets the new triggers or constraints at its next start. Tests: 8 cases added to `DataRulesTest` (an accepted ordinary row for every table, and each refusal), `DataRuleSqlTest` now expects 15 or more rules and checks the new columns exist in both schemas.

## 8. Done (for reference)
Quality of life (cleanup doc, section 4) items 1-10 are done. Items 6-9 (log no longer prints the default password, LIKE escaping in the order
history search, `orders.table_number` in the CREATE TABLE plus a checked add for old databases, the `LoginBackground.png` rename) were done after items 1-5;
see progress.md Session 33. Cleanup list steps 1-3 (dead code, unused dependencies, bugs) are done as well.

**Checked in the 3rd scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- No SQL is built from user input: every value goes through a `PreparedStatement` parameter. The few concatenated statements use fixed table names or ids read from the database itself.
- Password verification compares the hashes in constant time, a nonexistent username costs one hash, and the locked-account message only appears after the right password.
- Money is stored as whole dong (integer), foreign keys are on, and each multi-step write (payment, cancel, cleanup) runs in one transaction.
- The report window and the backup button check for an admin account; cancelling a paid order is admin-only.
- Passwords are never written to the log (the default admin password line was removed).

**Checked in the 4th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- User-entered text never reaches a file path, an FXML file or a style sheet; the export file name is built from dates only.
- The report window is admin-only and the account lock refuses to lock the signed-in account (the last-admin race is item 2A.2).
- The report export uses a BOM and `\r\n`, so Excel shows Vietnamese correctly.

**Checked in the 5th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- `VACUUM INTO` gets its path with single quotes doubled, so a folder name with an apostrophe cannot break or inject into the statement (the other problems with `backupTo` are bug 2.23).
- Payment is guarded against double payment: `payExistingOrder` only updates a row that is still `OPEN`, and a second attempt gets a clear message and changes nothing. Stock cannot go below zero (`stock >= ?` in the same transaction).
- Cancelling a paid order restocks, takes back points and changes the status in one transaction and rolls back on any error.
- The idle `Async` threads are daemons, the ordered queue is one thread (autosave then payment cannot overtake each other), and the ordered-queue name in the code is `runOrdered`.
- No log line I found writes a phone number, a name or a password.
- Not scanned in depth in this round (so no claim either way): the CSS files, the layout of every FXML file, `OrderHistoryWindow` and `ReportWindow` beyond what earlier scans covered.


**Checked in the 6th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- Every `onAction`/`onMouse...="#handler"` in the nine FXML files has a matching method in its controller (0 missing); every `<stylesheets>` and image path in the FXML files points to a file that exists.
- Deleting a product that has sales is blocked by the screen with a clear offer to stop selling it instead (`countOrderReferences`); the DAO alone would fail with a foreign-key error (reproduced: "Failed to delete product"), which is the intended last defence.
- Customer phone numbers are normalised inside `CustomerDAO` (insert, update, `existsByPhone`): `+84912345678` was stored as `0912345678`, so the customer dialog cannot create the same person under two spellings.
- The login screen runs the password check off the UI thread, trims only the username (not the password) and shows one message for a wrong name or password.
- The history search escapes `%` and `_`, sorting and paging happen in SQL, and the cancel button is re-checked for admin on click (not only when the row is selected).
- The report export writes a BOM and `\r\n` and shows the saved path; the report and its totals are admin-only.
- What I could not do: view any screen (no display here), so the visual look of every FXML/CSS screen, the 1366x768 fit, and the Windows glyphs remain for the human checks in section 0.


**Checked in the 7th scan and found fine (no action needed):**
- Restore cannot write outside the pictures folder: `extractImages` (called `replaceImages` before the 2.42 fix) normalises every zip entry path and skips one that does not start with `images/`. *(25th scan: re-read, the check `file.startsWith(root)` is there.)*
- A backup is written to `<name>.zip.part` and moved into place at the end, always ends in `.zip` (so it can never be `lunavera.db`), and automatic pruning touches only `auto-*.zip`; this matches what 2.8 says.
- Every SQL value in the DAOs I read (users, orders, customers, products, employees, tables, report) goes through a `PreparedStatement` parameter; the history `ORDER BY` is a whitelist; the few concatenated statements use fixed names or ids read from the database.
- Login: a nonexistent username costs one hash (`dummyHash`), a locked account is reported only after the right password, and `PasswordChangeService` checks the current password before a voluntary change.
- Payment: the discount rule is checked in the dialog and again by `DiscountPolicy`, a transfer payment cannot be completed without the tick box, and stock cannot go below zero inside the payment transaction.
- Idle logout closes every other window and the forced password dialog (hiding it ends the session cleanly).
- Read in a second round: `CategoryDAO`, `MenuSeeder`, `ScreenFit`, `CustomerController`'s dialog. Still not scanned in depth: the CSS and FXML layouts, `QrCode` internals, `MenuSeedData`, `PagedTable`/`PageBar`/`DashboardWidgets`, and the content of the tests (only their names were compared with the classes).
